Perfect! I now have a comprehensive understanding of the codebase. Let me create a detailed summary document.

## Comprehensive DuckDB Data Agent Codebase Exploration

### 1. PROJECT OVERVIEW & PURPOSE

**DuckDB Data Agent** is an AI-powered data analysis application that combines natural language processing with SQL analytics. The project consists of:
- **Agent Mode**: Ask questions in plain English; Claude AI writes and executes SQL queries
- **Editor Mode**: Direct SQL querying with an interactive results table
- Built with **FastAPI** backend + **React** frontend, powered by **DuckDB** (in-memory database)

Key differentiator: **Built entirely with Claude Code** (99%+ AI-generated, human-reviewed)

---

### 2. ARCHITECTURE & TECH STACK

#### **Backend Stack**
- **Framework**: FastAPI + Uvicorn
- **Database**: DuckDB (Python, in-memory)
- **Agent SDK**: Anthropic Agent SDK (Python)
- **MCP Support**: Model Context Protocol with SSE transport
- **Container Runtime**: Docker + gVisor (optional)
- **Observability**: Langfuse (optional)
- **Key Dependencies**: 
  - `fastapi`, `uvicorn`, `duckdb`, `python-dotenv`
  - `claude-agent-sdk`, `langfuse`, `langsmith`
  - `docker` (for container isolation)
  - `openpyxl` (Excel support)

#### **Frontend Stack**
- **Framework**: React 18 + TypeScript
- **Build Tool**: Vite
- **Styling**: CSS (custom components)
- **Markdown**: react-markdown + remark-gfm
- **State Management**: React Context API + custom hooks

#### **Sidecar (Optional Container Runtime)**
- **Language**: TypeScript/Node.js 20
- **Server**: Express
- **Agent SDK**: `@anthropic-ai/claude-agent-sdk` (TypeScript)
- **CLI**: `@anthropic-ai/claude-code` (required by SDK)
- **Tracing**: Langfuse

---

### 3. TOOL DEFINITIONS & REGISTRATION

#### **Tool Architecture**

**Location**: `/backend/app/tools.py`

The system uses the **Model Context Protocol (MCP)** for tool exposure:

```python
def create_duckdb_server(db: Database):
    @tool(
        "execute_sql",
        "Execute a SQL query against the DuckDB database...",
        {"sql": str},
    )
    async def execute_sql(args: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:
        # Executes SQL and returns columns, rows, rowCount (max 100 rows)
        ...
    
    return create_sdk_mcp_server(
        name="duckdb",
        version="1.0.0",
        tools=[execute_sql],
    )
```

**Tool Details**:
- **Single tool**: `execute_sql` - accepts SQL string, returns JSON with results
- **Max rows**: 100 per response (defined as `MAX_RESULT_ROWS`)
- **Error handling**: Returns `{"status": "error", "error": str(e)}`
- **Success format**: `{"status": "success", "columns": [...], "rows": [...], "rowCount": int}`

#### **Tool Registration Flow**

1. **Subprocess mode** (default):
   - `agent.py::stream_chat()` creates MCP server with `create_duckdb_server(db)`
   - Passes to `ClaudeSDKClient` as `mcp_servers={"duckdb": duckdb_server}`
   - Allowed tools: `["mcp__duckdb__execute_sql"]`

2. **Container mode** (optional):
   - `agent.py::_stream_chat_container()` exposes DuckDB via HTTP MCP SSE bridge
   - Container connects to `/mcp/sse?session_id=...` endpoint
   - Tool calls routed through `backend/app/mcp_sse.py`

---

### 4. AGENT RESPONSE FORMAT & STREAMING

#### **Agent Loop: Backend (`agent.py`)**

**Function**: `async def stream_chat(...) -> AsyncIterator[str]`

**Two execution paths**:
1. **Subprocess (default)**: Uses Anthropic Agent SDK directly
2. **Container (optional)**: Spawns gVisor sandbox container, calls sidecar HTTP endpoint

**Streaming Output Format**: Server-Sent Events (SSE)

```
event: thinking
data: {"text": "I need to..."}

event: thinking_done
data: {}

event: tool_call
data: {"id": "tool_123", "name": "execute_sql", "sql": "SELECT..."}

event: tool_result
data: {"id": "tool_123", "columns": [...], "rows": [...], "rowCount": 5}

event: answer
data: {"text": "The data shows..."}

event: done
data: {"session_id": "abc-123-def"}
```

**Event Types**:
- `thinking`: Extended thinking blocks (streamed token-by-token)
- `thinking_done`: Marker when thinking completes
- `tool_call`: Tool invocation with input
- `tool_result`: Tool execution result (SQL results or errors)
- `answer`: Final AI response text
- `error`: Error message
- `done`: Stream completion + session ID

#### **Message Streaming Details**

```python
# From agent.py - Subprocess path
if isinstance(msg, StreamEvent):
    # Token-level streaming via include_partial_messages=True
    if delta_type == "thinking_delta":
        yield f"event: thinking\ndata: {json.dumps({'text': text})}\n\n"
    elif delta_type == "text_delta":
        yield f"event: answer\ndata: {json.dumps({'text': text})}\n\n"

elif isinstance(msg, AssistantMessage):
    # Full message parsed from SDK
    for block in msg.content:
        if isinstance(block, ToolUseBlock):
            yield f"event: tool_call\ndata: {json.dumps({'id': block.id, 'name': ...})}\n\n"
```

**Key Capabilities**:
- **Token-level streaming**: `include_partial_messages=True` for real-time text display
- **Visible reasoning**: Thinking blocks expandable in UI
- **Tool tracing**: Each SQL execution visible with input and output
- **Session persistence**: `--resume` flag enables conversation resumption across requests
- **Error propagation**: Tool errors sent as `{"error": "..."}` in tool_result events

---

### 5. FRONTEND RENDERING & DATA DISPLAY

#### **Agent Service (`frontend/src/agent/agentService.ts`)**

Handles SSE stream parsing and event dispatch:

```typescript
async function streamSSE(
  response: Response,
  callbacks: AgentCallbacks,
  signal?: AbortSignal,
): Promise<void>
```

**Callbacks triggered**:
- `onTextChunk(text: string)` - Accumulate response text
- `onThinkingDone()` - Signal thinking completion
- `onToolCall(pending: ToolCallResult)` - Show pending tool
- `onToolResult(result: ToolCallResult)` - Show tool output
- `onDone(sessionId: string | null)` - Signal completion
- `onError(error: string)` - Handle errors

#### **State Management (`AgentContext.tsx`)**

React Context provides:
- `messages: ChatMessage[]` - Full conversation history
- `isStreaming: boolean` - Active request indicator
- `sendMessage(text)` - Send new query
- `editMessage(index, newContent)` - Edit and resend
- `deleteMessage(index)` - Rewind conversation
- `clearMessages()` - Reset chat

**Internal State Tracking**:
```typescript
// Segment-based rendering (thinking → tool calls → answer)
segmentsRef.current: ContentSegment[] = [
  { type: 'thinking', text: '...' },
  { type: 'tool', toolResult: {...} },
  { type: 'answer', text: '...' },
]

// Buffering for smooth streaming (50ms flush interval)
textBufferRef.current: string
flushTimerRef.current: NodeJS.Timeout | null
```

#### **UI Components**

| Component | Purpose |
|-----------|---------|
| **AgentPanel.tsx** | Main chat container, header, message list |
| **ChatInput.tsx** | Multi-line textarea with Ctrl/Cmd+Enter to send |
| **MessageBubble.tsx** | Renders message with segments (thinking/tools/answer) |
| **InlineQueryResult.tsx** | Renders tool results (SQL tables, errors, bash output) |
| **MessageBubble.css** | Styling for collapsible thinking blocks |
| **InlineQueryResult.css** | Table styling, error highlighting |

#### **Result Rendering: InlineQueryResult.tsx**

```typescript
// SQL Query Results
if (isSQLTool && hasStructuredData) {
  // <table> with columns as <th>, up to 20 rows displayed
  // Shows: "Row count: X (showing Y of Z)"
}

// Errors
if (result.error) {
  <div className="inline-query__error">{result.error}</div>
}

// Non-SQL tool output
if (result.output) {
  <div className="inline-query__output"><pre>{result.output}</pre></div>
}
```

**Visual Features**:
- Collapsible thinking blocks (HTML `<details>`)
- Color-coded tool labels (SQL = blue, bash = red, generic = gray)
- Inline result tables with fixed-width columns
- Row count display with truncation indicator
- Error states with red borders

### 8. DATA FLOW DIAGRAM

#### **Chat Message Flow (Subprocess Mode)**

```
Browser
  ↓ POST /api/chat (SSE) with message
Backend (FastAPI)
  ↓ Validates session (X-Session-ID header)
  ↓ Creates credential proxy token (UUID)
SessionManager
  ↓ Returns per-user DuckDB instance
Agent Loop (stream_chat)
  ↓ Launches Claude SDK subprocess with:
    - system_prompt (list of tables)
    - mcp_servers={"duckdb": execute_sql tool}
    - proxy credentials (ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL, ANTHROPIC_API_KEY)
  ↓ SDK streams response with include_partial_messages=True
  ├─ Thinking delta events → "event: thinking"
  ├─ Tool calls → "event: tool_call"
  │   ↓ execute_sql tool called
  │   ↓ db.execute_query_async(sql)
  │   ↓ Returns columns, rows
  │   ↑ "event: tool_result"
  ├─ Answer delta events → "event: answer"
  └─ Done → "event: done"
  ↓ Frontend processes SSE events
Frontend (React)
  ↓ AgentContext callbacks
  ├─ onTextChunk() → buffer + debounce flush
  ├─ onToolCall() → add pending segment
  ├─ onToolResult() → merge result into segment
  └─ onDone() → mark streaming false
  ↓ MessageBubble renders segments
  ├─ ThinkingBlock (collapsible <details>)
  │   └─ Thinking text + InlineQueryResult (tools)
  └─ Answer text
```

#### **File Upload Flow**

```
Browser → Frontend FileUpload component
  ↓ Drag/drop or click to select files
  ↓ POST /api/session/upload with FormData
Backend
  ↓ SessionManager validates session
  ↓ Database.load_csv/json/parquet/excel()
  │   ├─ Write to temp file
  │   ├─ DuckDB read_csv_auto/read_json_auto/etc
  │   └─ CREATE OR REPLACE TABLE
  ↓ get_table_info() → schema + row count
  ↓ POST /api/tables
Frontend
  ↓ refreshTables() in Sidebar
  ↓ Display table list with columns
```

---

### 9. SESSION MANAGEMENT

#### **Per-User Session Isolation**

```python
# backend/app/session_manager.py
class SessionManager:
    def get_or_create_session(session_id: str) -> Database:
        # Returns in-memory DuckDB instance per X-Session-ID header
        # Sessions auto-cleanup after 5 minutes of inactivity
```

**Session Lifecycle**:
1. Frontend generates UUID `X-Session-ID` header (browser-side)
2. All requests include this header
3. Backend maps to isolated DuckDB instance
4. Upload data, run queries → isolated to this session
5. Different browser tabs = different sessions
6. Idle sessions cleaned up every 60 seconds

#### **Agent Session Resumption**

```python
# agent.py
options = ClaudeAgentOptions(
    resume=session_id,  # Resume existing agent session
    ...
)
# Allows multi-turn conversations within a single agent session
```

---

### 10. SECURITY FEATURES

#### **Credential Proxy**

**Problem**: Agent subprocess could exfiltrate `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` from environment

**Solution**:
```
Claude Code subprocess
  ↓ ANTHROPIC_API_KEY=<short-lived UUID token>
  ↓ ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL=http://127.0.0.1:{PORT}/anthropic
  ↓ POST /anthropic/v1/messages
Backend (/anthropic reverse proxy)
  ↓ Validates UUID token (ProxyTokenStore)
  ↓ Swaps for real ANTHROPIC_API_KEY
  ↓ Forwards to api.anthropic.com
  ↓ Returns response
Token lifetime: 10 minutes
Token revocation: Explicit in agent.py finally block
```

#### **Container Isolation (Optional)**

```
Per-session gVisor sandbox container
  ├─ Read-only root filesystem
  ├─ All Linux capabilities dropped
  ├─ No host filesystem/Docker socket access
  ├─ Resource limits (CPU, memory, lifetime)
  ├─ Internal networks blocked (prevents metadata access)
  └─ Process/network isolation per container
```

---

### 11. VISUALIZATION & DATA DISPLAY PATTERNS

#### **Result Display Hierarchy**

1. **Structured SQL Results** (InlineQueryResult.tsx):
   - `<table>` with columns as `<th>`
   - Rows as `<tr>` with `<td>` cells
   - Max 20 rows visible (others truncated)
   - Row count + "showing X of Y" indicator

2. **Markdown Content** (MessageBubble.tsx):
   - React-Markdown with remark-gfm (GitHub flavored)
   - Used for thinking blocks and final answers
   - Supports code blocks, lists, emphasis

3. **Thinking Blocks** (collapsible `<details>`):
   - Summary shows last thinking line (preview)
   - Body reveals: full thinking + inline tool results
   - Auto-expands while thinking, collapses when done

4. **Error States**:
   - Red border on InlineQueryResult component
   - Error message text displayed
   - SQL input shown for context

5. **Tool Labels**:
   - SQL tools: Blue badge
   - Bash tools: Red badge
   - Generic tools: Gray badge

#### **Streaming UI Updates**

- **Text buffering**: Accumulate chunks, flush every 50ms (smooth animation)
- **Segment-based**: Separate thinking/tool/answer for clear visual hierarchy
- **Pending states**: Show tool input immediately, replace with results when ready
- **Scroll management**: Auto-scroll to bottom unless user scrolled up

---

### 12. OBSERVABILITY & TRACING

#### **Langfuse Integration (Optional)**

When configured with `LANGFUSE_PUBLIC_KEY` + `LANGFUSE_SECRET_KEY`:

```python
# backend/app/tracing.py
langfuse = Langfuse()

# In agent.py
observation_ctx = langfuse.start_as_current_observation(
    name="agent-chat",
    input={"message": message, "conversation_history": ...},
    metadata={"model": ANTHROPIC_MODEL},
)
# Traces: LLM calls, tool invocations, execution time
langfuse.update_current_trace(session_id=trace_session_id)
```

**Trace Metadata**:
- Message content (first 500 chars)
- Conversation history
- Model name
- Session ID (for grouping turns)
- Tool call inputs/outputs
- Execution timing

**Frontend**: Langfuse Traces button links to dashboard when enabled

### 13. CONFIGURATION SUMMARY

#### **Environment Variables** (`backend/app/config.py`)

| Variable | Default | Purpose |
|----------|---------|---------|
| `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` | (required) | API key for Claude |
| `ANTHROPIC_MODEL` | `claude-opus-4-6` | Model selection |
| `PROXY_BASE_URL` | `http://127.0.0.1:8000` | Credential proxy address |
| `MAX_TOTAL_SIZE_BYTES` | 500 MB | File upload limit |
| `LANGFUSE_PUBLIC_KEY` | "" | Tracing (optional) |
| `LANGFUSE_SECRET_KEY` | "" | Tracing (optional) |
| `LANGFUSE_BASE_URL` | `https://cloud.langfuse.com` | Tracing endpoint |
| `CONTAINER_ENABLED` | `false` | Enable gVisor sandbox |
| `CONTAINER_IMAGE` | `duckdb-agent-sidecar:latest` | Sidecar Docker image |
| `CONTAINER_RUNTIME` | `runc` | Docker runtime (runc/runsc) |
| `CONTAINER_MEMORY_LIMIT` | `512m` | Memory per container |
| `CONTAINER_CPU_LIMIT` | `0.5` | CPU per container |
| `CONTAINER_MAX_LIFETIME_SECONDS` | `600` | Max container lifetime |
| `CONTAINER_NETWORK` | `agent-sandbox` | Docker network name |
| `CORS_ALLOWED_ORIGINS` | `http://localhost:5173,http://localhost:8000` | CORS whitelist |

---

### 14. KEY PATTERNS & DESIGN DECISIONS

1. **SSE for Streaming**: Token-level streaming via Server-Sent Events (text/event-stream)
2. **Segment-based rendering**: Separate thinking/tools/answer for UX clarity
3. **Buffered text updates**: 50ms flush interval prevents excessive re-renders
4. **MCP for tool exposure**: Model Context Protocol for clean tool abstraction
5. **Per-user session isolation**: Separate in-memory DuckDB per browser tab
6. **Credential proxy**: UUID tokens instead of exposing real API keys
7. **Optional containerization**: Subprocess by default, gVisor sandbox on opt-in
8. **Message editing**: Rebuild with conversation history as context
9. **Message deletion**: Rewind conversation + flag for new Langfuse session
10. **Langfuse tracing**: Optional observability, zero overhead when disabled

---

This comprehensive exploration covers the full stack: project purpose, tool definitions, agent streaming, frontend rendering, API structure, session management, security, data flow, and configuration patterns. The codebase exemplifies clean separation between backend (agent logic + database) and frontend (UI state + streaming), with sophisticated streaming and context management for a smooth user experience.

Good — I have a solid picture of the project. Here's a quick summary before my first question:

- **Backend**: FastAPI + DuckDB + Claude SDK (MCP tools in `tools.py`)
- **Frontend**: React + TypeScript, renders tool results via `InlineQueryResult.tsx`
- **Streaming**: SSE events (`tool_call`, `tool_result`, `answer`)
- **Current display**: SQL result tables, markdown text — no charts yet

**First clarifying question:**

Where should charts be rendered? Three approaches are possible:

| Option | How it works | Trade-offs |
|--------|-------------|------------|
| **A. Client-side library** (e.g. Plotly.js, Recharts) | Agent returns a chart spec (JSON); frontend renders it interactively | Interactive, no extra backend deps, fits existing React stack well — **recommended** |
| **B. Server-side image** (e.g. matplotlib, plotly-python) | Backend generates a PNG/SVG; agent returns base64 image embedded in the response | Simpler frontend changes, but static, adds heavy Python deps, no interactivity |
| **C. Agent generates HTML/JS snippet** | Agent writes a self-contained chart snippet; frontend renders in an iframe or sandbox | Most flexible but security concerns, hard to style consistently |

Which approach do you prefer?

> DEVELOPER

approach a

> AGENT

Great choice. Next question:

**Which charting library should we use on the frontend?**

| Option | Description | Trade-offs |
|--------|-------------|------------|
| **A. Plotly.js** (`react-plotly.js`) | Declarative JSON spec, 40+ chart types, interactive by default | Large bundle (~3MB), but very LLM-friendly spec format — **recommended** |
| **B. Recharts** | React-native composable components, lighter (~500KB) | Fewer chart types, spec is more React-component-oriented (harder for LLM to generate cleanly) |
| **C. Chart.js** (`react-chartjs-2`) | Widely used, moderate bundle (~200KB) | Less declarative than Plotly, LLM needs to know Chart.js config API |
| **D. Vega-Lite** (`react-vega`) | Powerful grammar-of-graphics JSON spec | Steeper learning curve for LLM, less common in training data |

I recommend **Plotly.js** — its fully declarative JSON spec (data + layout as plain objects) is ideal for an LLM to generate without needing to know React component APIs. The agent can produce a complete chart spec in one shot.

> DEVELOPER

option a

> AGENT

Plotly.js it is. Next question:

**What chart types should the agent be able to generate?**

| Option | Included types |
|--------|---------------|
| **A. Core set** (recommended) | Bar, line, scatter, pie — covers ~90% of analytical use cases, simpler system prompt |
| **B. Extended set** | Core + histogram, heatmap, box plot, area chart — more versatile |
| **C. Full Plotly suite** | Everything Plotly supports (waterfall, treemap, funnel, 3D, etc.) — agent decides freely |

I recommend **A (core set)** — keeping the supported types small means the system prompt stays tight and the agent is less likely to hallucinate unsupported chart configs. We can always expand later.

> DEVELOPER

c

> AGENT

Full Plotly suite — the agent gets maximum flexibility. One more question:

**How should the `generate_chart` tool work mechanically?**

The agent already calls `execute_sql` to fetch data. Then what?

| Option | Tool input | How chart gets to the frontend |
|--------|-----------|-------------------------------|
| **A. Agent builds full Plotly spec** (recommended) | Agent passes complete `{data: [...], layout: {...}}` JSON to the tool | Tool echoes the spec back as a `tool_result`; frontend detects it and renders a Plotly chart instead of a table |
| **B. Agent passes data + chart config** | Agent passes `{chart_type, x_col, y_col, title, ...}` and the backend constructs the Plotly spec | Backend owns the spec-building logic; less flexible but more constrained |
| **C. Chart spec embedded in answer text** | Agent writes a fenced JSON block in its answer text | Frontend parses answer text looking for chart blocks; fragile, breaks markdown |

I recommend **A** — the agent constructs the full Plotly spec directly. This gives the full Plotly suite (your choice C above) naturally, keeps the backend dead-simple (just pass-through), and the frontend just needs to detect the `chart_spec` key in `tool_result` to switch rendering from table to Plotly chart.

---

## Design: Chart Generation Tool

### Section 1 — Architecture Overview

The feature adds one new MCP tool (`generate_chart`) and extends the frontend to render Plotly charts inline inside tool result bubbles. No new backend services or endpoints needed.

**Data flow:**
```
User: "show me a bar chart of sales by region"
  │
  ├─ Agent calls execute_sql → gets tabular data
  │
  ├─ Agent calls generate_chart({ data: [...], layout: {...} })
  │     └─ Backend validates spec has a `data` array, echoes it back
  │
  └─ Frontend SSE receives tool_result with chart_spec key
        └─ InlineQueryResult renders <ChartWidget> (react-plotly.js)
              instead of the usual <table>
```

Does this architecture look right so far?

> DEVELOPER

yes

> AGENT

### Section 2 — Backend Changes

**`backend/app/tools.py` — new `generate_chart` MCP tool:**

```python
@tool(
    "generate_chart",
    "Generate a Plotly chart from a JSON spec. Call this after execute_sql "
    "to visualize data. Pass a complete Plotly figure spec with 'data' (array "
    "of traces) and optional 'layout' object.",
    {
        "data": list,      # Plotly traces array (required)
        "layout": dict,    # Plotly layout object (optional)
    },
)
async def generate_chart(args: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:
    if not args.get("data"):
        return {"status": "error", "error": "Missing required field: data"}
    return {
        "status": "success",
        "chart_spec": {
            "data": args["data"],
            "layout": args.get("layout", {}),
        },
    }
```

- Validation is intentionally minimal — Plotly is forgiving and the agent constructs the spec
- No data transformation on the backend; it's pure pass-through after the shape check

**`backend/app/agent.py` — system prompt addition:**

A short section appended to the existing system prompt:

```
## Chart Generation
After running a SQL query, if a chart would help the user understand the data,
call the generate_chart tool with a valid Plotly figure spec:
- `data`: array of Plotly trace objects (bar, scatter, pie, heatmap, etc.)
- `layout`: optional layout object (title, axis labels, etc.)
Build the chart data directly from the SQL results. Full Plotly trace types are supported.
```

Does this backend design look right?

> DEVELOPER

yes

> AGENT

### Section 3 — Frontend Changes

**`frontend/src/types.ts` — extend `ToolCallResult`:**

```typescript
export interface ToolCallResult {
  // ... existing fields ...
  chart_spec?: {
    data: Plotly.Data[];
    layout?: Partial<Plotly.Layout>;
  };
}
```

**New `frontend/src/components/ChartWidget.tsx`:**

```tsx
import Plot from 'react-plotly.js';

interface ChartWidgetProps {
  data: Plotly.Data[];
  layout?: Partial<Plotly.Layout>;
}

export function ChartWidget({ data, layout }: ChartWidgetProps) {
  return (
    <Plot
      data={data}
      layout={{ autosize: true, ...layout }}
      useResizeHandler
      style={{ width: '100%' }}
      config={{ responsive: true, displayModeBar: true }}
    />
  );
}
```

**`frontend/src/components/InlineQueryResult.tsx` — add chart branch:**

```tsx
// At the top of the render, before the table branch:
if (result.chart_spec) {
  return (
    <div className="inline-query inline-query--chart">
      <ChartWidget
        data={result.chart_spec.data}
        layout={result.chart_spec.layout}
      />
    </div>
  );
}
```

**`frontend/package.json` — new dependency:**

```
react-plotly.js  +  plotly.js-dist-min  (lighter Plotly bundle, ~1.5MB vs 3MB)
@types/react-plotly.js
```

`agentService.ts` needs no changes — `chart_spec` flows through the existing `tool_result` SSE event parsing automatically since it already passes the full parsed JSON into `ToolCallResult`.

Does this frontend design look right?

> DEVELOPER

yes

     1→import json
     2→from typing import Any
     3→from claude_agent_sdk import tool, create_sdk_mcp_server
     4→from app.database import Database
     5→
     6→MAX_RESULT_ROWS = 100
     7→
     8→
     9→def create_duckdb_server(db: Database):
    10→    @tool(
    11→        "execute_sql",
    12→        "Execute a SQL query against the DuckDB database. Use this to query loaded tables, "
    13→        "create views, or run any valid DuckDB SQL. Results are returned as JSON with columns, "
    14→        "rows, and rowCount.",
    15→        {"sql": str},
    16→    )
    17→    async def execute_sql(args: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:
    18→        sql = args["sql"]
    19→        try:
    20→            result = await db.execute_query_async(sql)
    21→            truncated_rows = result["rows"][:MAX_RESULT_ROWS]
    22→            result_json = {
    23→                "status": "success",
    24→                "columns": result["columns"],
    25→                "rows": truncated_rows,
    26→                "rowCount": result["rowCount"],
    27→            }
    28→            content_text = json.dumps(result_json, default=str)
    29→            return {"content": [{"type": "text", "text": content_text}]}
    30→        except Exception as e:
    31→            error_json = {"status": "error", "error": str(e)}
    32→            return {
    33→                "content": [{"type": "text", "text": json.dumps(error_json)}],
    34→                "is_error": True,
    35→            }
    36→
    37→    return create_sdk_mcp_server(
    38→        name="duckdb",
    39→        version="1.0.0",
    40→        tools=[execute_sql],
    41→    )
    42→

    25→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)

    27→# Monkey-patch parse_message to handle unknown message types (e.g. rate_limit_event)
    28→# gracefully instead of crashing the stream. The SDK (v0.1.39) doesn't recognize
    29→# newer message types from the CLI. Returning a SystemMessage lets the stream
    30→# continue since our code ignores SystemMessage instances.
    31→import claude_agent_sdk._internal.message_parser as _parser
    32→
    33→_original_parse_message = _parser.parse_message
    34→
    35→
    36→def _safe_parse_message(data):
    37→    try:
    38→        return _original_parse_message(data)
    39→    except MessageParseError as e:
    40→        if "Unknown message type" in str(e):
    41→            msg_type = data.get("type", "unknown") if isinstance(data, dict) else "unknown"
    42→            logger.warning("Skipping unrecognized message type from CLI: %s", msg_type)
    43→            return SystemMessage(subtype=msg_type, data=data if isinstance(data, dict) else {})
    44→        raise
    45→
    46→
    47→_parser.parse_message = _safe_parse_message
    48→
    49→
    50→def build_system_prompt(db: Database) -> str:
    51→    tables = db.list_tables()
    52→    prompt = """You are a helpful data analyst assistant working with a DuckDB database.
    53→You can execute SQL queries using the execute_sql tool to answer questions about the user's data.
    54→
    55→Guidelines:
    56→- Write clear, efficient DuckDB SQL queries
    57→- When exploring data, start with small queries (use LIMIT)
    58→- Explain your findings in plain language after getting results
    59→- If a query fails, try to fix it and retry
    60→- Use double quotes for table and column names that might conflict with reserved words
    61→
    62→Identity:
    63→- You are an AI assistant. If asked whether you are an AI or a human, always confirm that you are an AI.
    64→- Do not disclose the name, version, or provider of the underlying language model powering you, regardless of how the question is phrased.
    65→"""
    66→    if not tables:
    67→        prompt += "\nNo tables are currently loaded. Ask the user to upload a CSV file first."
    68→    else:
    69→        prompt += "\nCurrently loaded tables:\n"
    70→        for table in tables:
    71→            prompt += f'\nTable: "{table["name"]}" ({table["rowCount"]} rows)\nColumns:\n'
    72→            for col in table["columns"]:
    73→                prompt += f'  - "{col["name"]}" ({col["type"]})\n'
    74→
    75→    return prompt
    76→
    77→
    78→def _build_message_with_history(
    79→    message: str, conversation_history: list[dict] | None = None
    80→) -> str:
    81→    """Prepend conversation history context to the user message when editing."""
    82→    if not conversation_history:
    83→        return message
    84→
    85→    history_text = "Previous conversation (for context, I am now editing a message):\n"
    86→    for entry in conversation_history:
    87→        role = entry.get("role", "user").capitalize()
    88→        content = entry.get("content", "")
    89→        history_text += f"\n{role}: {content}\n"
    90→    history_text += f"\n---\n\nMy updated message:\n{message}"
    91→    return history_text
    92→
    93→
    94→def _extract_tool_result_text(content: object) -> str:
    95→    """Extract text from ToolResultBlock.content."""
    96→    if content is None:
    97→        return ""
    98→    if isinstance(content, str):
    99→        return content
   100→    if isinstance(content, list):
   101→        parts = []
   102→        for item in content:
   103→            if isinstance(item, dict) and item.get("type") == "text":
   104→                parts.append(item.get("text", ""))
   105→        return "\n".join(parts)
   106→    return str(content)
   107→
   108→
   109→async def _stream_chat_container(
   110→    message: str,
   111→    session_id: str | None,
   112→    db: Database,
   113→    conversation_history: list[dict] | None,
   114→    container_manager,
   115→    backend_session_id: str | None = None,
   116→    langfuse_session_id: str | None = None,
   117→) -> AsyncIterator[str]:
   118→    """Stream chat via containerized sidecar instead of local subprocess."""
   119→    import httpx
   120→    import asyncio
   121→
   122→    query_message = _build_message_with_history(message, conversation_history)
   123→    system_prompt = build_system_prompt(db)
   124→
   125→    session_token=[REDACTED].create_token()
   126→
   127→    # Pass Langfuse credentials to the container so the sidecar's
   128→    # TypeScript Langfuse SDK can create traces directly.
   129→    env: dict[str, str] = {
   130→        "ANTHROPIC_API_KEY": session_token,
   131→        "ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL": f"{PROXY_BASE_URL}/anthropic",
   132→    }
   133→    if LANGFUSE_ENABLED:
   134→        env["LANGFUSE_PUBLIC_KEY"] = LANGFUSE_PUBLIC_KEY
   135→        env["LANGFUSE_SECRET_KEY"] = LANGFUSE_SECRET_KEY
   136→        env["LANGFUSE_BASE_URL"] = LANGFUSE_BASE_URL
   137→
   138→    if "127.0.0.1" in PROXY_BASE_URL or "localhost" in PROXY_BASE_URL:
   139→        logger.warning(
   140→            "PROXY_BASE_URL=%s uses localhost which is unreachable from containers. "
   141→            "Set PROXY_BASE_URL to the host's Docker-accessible address "
   142→            "(e.g., http://host.docker.internal:10000).",
   143→            PROXY_BASE_URL,
   144→        )
   145→
   146→    # Use the backend session ID (X-Session-ID header) for both:
   147→    # 1. MCP SSE URL — so the container queries the correct DuckDB instance
   148→    # 2. Container lifecycle key — so the same container is reused across
   149→    #    requests from the same browser tab (the Claude agent session_id
   150→    #    changes after the first response, which would orphan the container)
   151→    stable_session = backend_session_id or session_id or "default"
   152→
   153→    try:
   154→        info = container_manager.create(stable_session, env)
   155→
   156→        # Send SSE keepalive comments during container startup to prevent
   157→        # proxy buffering / idle-connection timeouts (Vite, nginx, etc.).
   158→        # SSE comments (lines starting with ':') are ignored by clients.
   159→        yield ": keepalive\n\n"
   160→
   161→        # Wait for container to be ready
   162→        for attempt in range(10):
   163→            try:
   164→                async with httpx.AsyncClient(timeout=httpx.Timeout(5.0)) as check_client:
   165→                    resp = await check_client.get(f"{info.url}/health")
   166→                    if resp.status_code == 200:
   167→                        break
   168→            except Exception:
   169→                pass
   170→            yield ": keepalive\n\n"
   171→            await asyncio.sleep(1)
   172→        else:
   173→            raise RuntimeError("Sidecar container failed health check after 10 attempts")
   174→
   175→        payload: dict = {
   176→            "message": query_message,
   177→            "session_id": session_id,
   178→            "system_prompt": system_prompt,
   179→            "model": ANTHROPIC_MODEL,
   180→            "mcp_server_url": f"{PROXY_BASE_URL}/mcp/sse?session_id={stable_session}",
   181→            "env": {
   182→                "ANTHROPIC_API_KEY": session_token,
   183→                "ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL": f"{PROXY_BASE_URL}/anthropic",
   184→            },
   185→        }
   186→        if langfuse_session_id:
   187→            payload["langfuse_session_id"] = langfuse_session_id
   188→        # Pass original message & history separately for Langfuse trace metadata
   189→        if conversation_history:
   190→            payload["original_message"] = message
   191→            payload["conversation_history"] = conversation_history
   192→
   193→        has_tool_calls = False
   194→        has_thinking = False
   195→        done_sent = False
   196→        tool_names: dict[str, str] = {}
   197→        tool_sqls: dict[str, str] = {}
   198→        actual_session_id = session_id
   199→
   200→        async with httpx.AsyncClient(timeout=httpx.Timeout(300.0)) as client:
   201→            async with client.stream("POST", f"{info.url}/query", json=payload) as response:
   202→                async for line in response.aiter_lines():
   203→                    if not line.startswith("data: "):
   204→                        continue
   205→                    raw = line[6:]
   206→                    try:
   207→                        msg = json.loads(raw)
   208→                    except json.JSONDecodeError:
   209→                        continue
   210→
   211→                    msg_type = msg.get("type")
   212→
   213→                    # --- Token-level streaming events from SDK ---
   214→                    if msg_type == "stream_event":
   215→                        event = msg.get("event", {})
   216→                        event_type = event.get("type", "")
   217→
   218→                        if event_type == "content_block_delta":
   219→                            delta = event.get("delta", {})
   220→                            delta_type = delta.get("type", "")
   221→                            if delta_type == "thinking_delta":
   222→                                text = delta.get("thinking", "")
   223→                                if text:
   224→                                    yield f"event: thinking\ndata: {json.dumps({'text': text})}\n\n"
   225→                            elif delta_type == "text_delta":
   226→                                text = delta.get("text", "")
   227→                                if text:
   228→                                    event_name = "answer" if has_tool_calls else "thinking"
   229→                                    yield f"event: {event_name}\ndata: {json.dumps({'text': text})}\n\n"
   230→
   231→                        elif event_type == "content_block_start":
   232→                            block = event.get("content_block", {})
   233→                            block_type = block.get("type")
   234→                            if block_type == "thinking":
   235→                                has_thinking = True
   236→                            elif block_type == "text":
   237→                                if has_thinking:
   238→                                    yield f"event: thinking_done\ndata: {json.dumps({})}\n\n"
   239→                            elif block_type == "tool_use":
   240→                                has_thinking = False
   241→                                has_tool_calls = True
   242→
   243→                    # --- Complete assistant message (contains tool_use blocks) ---
   244→                    elif msg_type == "assistant":
   245→                        message_obj = msg.get("message", {})
   246→                        for block in message_obj.get("content", []):
   247→                            block_type = block.get("type")
   248→                            if block_type == "tool_use":
   249→                                has_tool_calls = True
   250→                                tool_id = block.get("id", "")
   251→                                tool_name = block.get("name", "")
   252→                                tool_input = block.get("input", {})
   253→                                tool_names[tool_id] = tool_name
   254→                                sql = tool_input.get("sql", "")
   255→                                if sql:
   256→                                    tool_sqls[tool_id] = sql
   257→                                tool_call_data: dict = {"id": tool_id, "name": tool_name}
   258→                                if sql:
   259→                                    tool_call_data["sql"] = sql
   260→                                else:
   261→                                    tool_call_data["input"] = tool_input
   262→                                yield f"event: tool_call\ndata: {json.dumps(tool_call_data, default=str)}\n\n"
   263→
   264→                    # --- Tool results from user messages ---
   265→                    elif msg_type == "user":
   266→                        message_obj = msg.get("message", {})
   267→                        for block in message_obj.get("content", []):
   268→                            if block.get("type") != "tool_result":
   269→                                continue
   270→                            tool_id = block.get("tool_use_id", "")
   271→                            name = tool_names.get(tool_id, "")
   272→                            content_parts = block.get("content", [])
   273→                            text = ""
   274→                            if isinstance(content_parts, list):
   275→                                for part in content_parts:
   276→                                    if isinstance(part, dict) and part.get("type") == "text":
   277→                                        text = part.get("text", "")
   278→                            elif isinstance(content_parts, str):
   279→                                text = content_parts
   280→
   281→                            # Try to parse structured MCP result
   282→                            result_data: dict = {"id": tool_id, "name": name}
   283→                            # Include the SQL from the original tool_call
   284→                            original_sql = tool_sqls.get(tool_id, "")
   285→                            if original_sql:
   286→                                result_data["sql"] = original_sql
   287→                            try:
   288→                                parsed = json.loads(text)
   289→                                if parsed.get("status") == "success":
   290→                                    result_data["columns"] = parsed.get("columns", [])
   291→                                    result_data["rows"] = parsed.get("rows", [])[:100]
   292→                                    result_data["rowCount"] = parsed.get("rowCount", 0)
   293→                                elif parsed.get("status") == "error":
   294→                                    result_data["error"] = parsed.get("error", "")
   295→                                else:
   296→                                    result_data["output"] = text
   297→                            except (json.JSONDecodeError, AttributeError):
   298→                                result_data["output"] = text
   299→                            if block.get("is_error"):
   300→                                result_data["error"] = text
   301→                            yield f"event: tool_result\ndata: {json.dumps(result_data, default=str)}\n\n"
   302→
   303→                    # --- Final result ---
   304→                    elif msg_type == "result":
   305→                        actual_session_id = msg.get("session_id") or actual_session_id
   306→                        if msg.get("is_error"):
   307→                            errors = msg.get("errors", [])
   308→                            error_text = msg.get("result") or "; ".join(errors) or "Unknown error"
   309→                            yield f"event: error\ndata: {json.dumps({'message': error_text})}\n\n"
   310→                        yield f"event: done\ndata: {json.dumps({'session_id': actual_session_id})}\n\n"
   311→                        done_sent = True
   312→
   313→                    # --- Sidecar error (e.g. SDK/CLI crash inside container) ---
   314→                    elif msg_type == "error":
   315→                        error_text = msg.get("message") or "Sidecar error"
   316→                        logger.error("Sidecar reported error: %s", error_text)
   317→                        yield f"event: error\ndata: {json.dumps({'message': error_text})}\n\n"
   318→
   319→                    # --- Extract session_id early from system init ---
   320→                    elif msg_type == "system":
   321→                        sys_session = msg.get("session_id")
   322→                        if sys_session:
   323→                            actual_session_id = sys_session
   324→
   325→        # Guard: always send done even if sidecar ended without result message
   326→        if not done_sent:
   327→            logger.warning("Sidecar stream ended without result message; sending done event")
   328→            yield f"event: done\ndata: {json.dumps({'session_id': actual_session_id})}\n\n"
   329→
   330→    except Exception as e:
   331→        logger.error("Container agent error: %s", str(e))
   332→        yield f"event: error\ndata: {json.dumps({'message': str(e)})}\n\n"
   333→    finally:
   334→        proxy_token_store.revoke_token(session_token)
   335→        # Container intentionally kept alive for session resume (--resume flag).
   336→        # Containers are cleaned up by the background cleanup loop after
   337→        # CONTAINER_MAX_LIFETIME_SECONDS, or on application shutdown.
   338→
   339→
   340→async def stream_chat(
   341→    message: str,
   342→    session_id: str | None = None,
   343→    db: Database | None = None,
   344→    conversation_history: list[dict] | None = None,
   345→    langfuse_session_id: str | None = None,
   346→    backend_session_id: str | None = None,
   347→) -> AsyncIterator[str]:
   348→    """Stream agent chat responses as SSE events."""
   349→    if CONTAINER_ENABLED:
   350→        from app.container_manager import container_manager
   351→        if container_manager is None:
   352→            logger.error(
   353→                "CONTAINER_ENABLED=true but Docker is not available. "
   354→                "Falling back to subprocess mode."
   355→            )
   356→        else:
   357→            async for event in _stream_chat_container(
   358→                message, session_id, db, conversation_history, container_manager,
   359→                backend_session_id=backend_session_id,
   360→                langfuse_session_id=langfuse_session_id,
   361→            ):
   362→                yield event
   363→            return
   364→
   365→    if db is None:
   366→        raise ValueError("db must be provided")
   367→    duckdb_server = create_duckdb_server(db)
   368→
   369→    logger.info("Using model: %s", ANTHROPIC_MODEL)
   370→
   371→    # Collect stderr from the CLI subprocess for debugging
   372→    stderr_lines: list[str] = []
   373→
   374→    # Use the --resume flag to continue an existing session
   375→    session_token=[REDACTED].create_token()
   376→    options = ClaudeAgentOptions(
   377→        model=ANTHROPIC_MODEL,
   378→        system_prompt=build_system_prompt(db),
   379→        mcp_servers={"duckdb": duckdb_server},
   380→        allowed_tools=["mcp__duckdb__execute_sql"],
   381→        permission_mode="bypassPermissions",
   382→        max_turns=20,
   383→        include_partial_messages=True,
   384→        stderr=lambda line: stderr_lines.append(line),
   385→        env={
   386→            "ANTHROPIC_API_KEY": session_token,
   387→            "ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL": f"{PROXY_BASE_URL}/anthropic",
   388→            # Scrub Langfuse credentials so the agent subprocess cannot
   389→            # read them from the inherited environment.
   390→            "LANGFUSE_PUBLIC_KEY": "",
   391→            "LANGFUSE_SECRET_KEY": "",
   392→        },
   393→        **({"resume": session_id} if session_id else {}),
   394→    )
   395→
   396→    # When editing, prepend conversation history to the user message
   397→    # instead of bloating the system prompt
   398→    query_message = _build_message_with_history(message, conversation_history)
   399→
   400→    client = ClaudeSDKClient(options=options)
   401→    # Will be set from the CLI's ResultMessage; use the passed-in value until then
   402→    actual_session_id = session_id
   403→
   404→    # --- Langfuse OTel tracing setup (conditional) ---
   405→    # Deferred: session_id is set after the CLI returns it in ResultMessage
   406→    langfuse = get_langfuse_client()
   407→    observation_ctx = None
   408→    propagate_ctx = None
   409→    if langfuse:
   410→        try:
   411→            trace_input: dict = {"message": message[:500]}
   412→            if conversation_history:
   413→                trace_input["conversation_history"] = conversation_history
   414→            observation_ctx = langfuse.start_as_current_observation(
   415→                name="agent-chat",
   416→                input=trace_input,
   417→                metadata={"model": ANTHROPIC_MODEL},
   418→            )
   419→            observation_ctx.__enter__()
   420→
   421→            # Propagate the stable conversation session_id for child spans
   422→            effective_langfuse_session_id = langfuse_session_id or session_id
   423→            if effective_langfuse_session_id:
   424→                from langfuse import propagate_attributes
   425→                propagate_ctx = propagate_attributes(session_id=effective_langfuse_session_id)
   426→                propagate_ctx.__enter__()
   427→        except Exception as e:
   428→            logger.debug("Failed to set up Langfuse tracing context: %s", e)
   429→            observation_ctx = None
   430→
   431→    try:
   432→        await client.connect()
   433→        await client.query(query_message, session_id=session_id or "default")
   434→
   435→        has_tool_calls = False
   436→        has_thinking = False
   437→        done_sent = False
   438→        sql_result_ids: set[str] = set()
   439→        tool_names: dict[str, str] = {}
   440→
   441→        async for msg in client.receive_response():
   442→            if isinstance(msg, StreamEvent):
   443→                event = msg.event
   444→                event_type = event.get("type", "")
   445→
   446→                if event_type == "content_block_delta":
   447→                    delta = event.get("delta", {})
   448→                    delta_type = delta.get("type", "")
   449→                    if delta_type == "thinking_delta":
   450→                        text = delta.get("thinking", "")
   451→                        if text:
   452→                            yield f"event: thinking\ndata: {json.dumps({'text': text})}\n\n"
   453→                    elif delta_type == "text_delta":
   454→                        text = delta.get("text", "")
   455→                        event_name = "thinking" if not has_tool_calls else "answer"
   456→                        yield f"event: {event_name}\ndata: {json.dumps({'text': text})}\n\n"
   457→
   458→                elif event_type == "content_block_start":
   459→                    block = event.get("content_block", {})
   460→                    block_type = block.get("type")
   461→                    if block_type == "thinking":
   462→                        has_thinking = True
   463→                    elif block_type == "text":
   464→                        if has_thinking:
   465→                            yield f"event: thinking_done\ndata: {json.dumps({})}\n\n"
   466→                    elif block_type == "tool_use":
   467→                        has_thinking = False
   468→                        has_tool_calls = True
   469→
   470→            elif isinstance(msg, AssistantMessage):
   471→                for block in msg.content:
   472→                    if isinstance(block, ToolUseBlock):
   473→                        has_tool_calls = True
   474→                        tool_name = getattr(block, "name", "") or ""
   475→                        tool_names[block.id] = tool_name
   476→                        sql = block.input.get("sql", "")
   477→                        command = block.input.get("command", "")
   478→
   479→                        # Emit tool_call for ALL tool types
   480→                        tool_call_data: dict = {"id": block.id, "name": tool_name}
   481→                        if sql:
   482→                            tool_call_data["sql"] = sql
   483→                        if command:
   484→                            tool_call_data["command"] = command
   485→                        if not sql and not command:
   486→                            tool_call_data["input"] = block.input
   487→                        yield f"event: tool_call\ndata: {json.dumps(tool_call_data, default=str)}\n\n"
   488→
   489→                        # For SQL tools, execute query for structured results
   490→                        if sql:
   491→                            sql_result_ids.add(block.id)
   492→                            try:
   493→                                result = db.execute_query(sql)
   494→                                truncated = result["rows"][:100]
   495→                                yield f"event: tool_result\ndata: {json.dumps({'id': block.id, 'name': tool_name, 'sql': sql, 'columns': result['columns'], 'rows': truncated, 'rowCount': result['rowCount']}, default=str)}\n\n"
   496→                            except Exception as e:
   497→                                yield f"event: tool_result\ndata: {json.dumps({'id': block.id, 'name': tool_name, 'sql': sql, 'error': str(e)})}\n\n"
   498→
   499→            elif isinstance(msg, UserMessage):
   500→                # Capture tool results from the SDK for non-SQL tools
   501→                content = msg.content
   502→                if isinstance(content, list):
   503→                    for block in content:
   504→                        if isinstance(block, ToolResultBlock):
   505→                            if block.tool_use_id in sql_result_ids:
   506→                                continue
   507→                            output = _extract_tool_result_text(block.content)
   508→                            name = tool_names.get(block.tool_use_id, "")
   509→                            result_data: dict = {
   510→                                "id": block.tool_use_id,
   511→                                "name": name,
   512→                                "output": output,
   513→                            }
   514→                            if block.is_error:
   515→                                result_data["error"] = output
   516→                            yield f"event: tool_result\ndata: {json.dumps(result_data, default=str)}\n\n"
   517→
   518→            elif isinstance(msg, ResultMessage):
   519→                # Use the CLI's session_id so --resume can find it
   520→                actual_session_id = msg.session_id or actual_session_id
   521→                if msg.is_error and msg.result:
   522→                    yield f"event: error\ndata: {json.dumps({'message': msg.result})}\n\n"
   523→                yield f"event: done\ndata: {json.dumps({'session_id': actual_session_id})}\n\n"
   524→                done_sent = True
   525→
   526→        # Guard: always send done even if SDK ended without ResultMessage
   527→        if not done_sent:
   528→            logger.warning("SDK stream ended without ResultMessage; sending done event")
   529→            yield f"event: done\ndata: {json.dumps({'session_id': actual_session_id})}\n\n"
   530→
   531→    except Exception as e:
   532→        error_msg = str(e)
   533→        if stderr_lines:
   534→            error_msg += f" | CLI stderr: {' '.join(stderr_lines[-5:])}"
   535→        logger.error("Agent error: %s", error_msg)
   536→        yield f"event: error\ndata: {json.dumps({'message': error_msg})}\n\n"
   537→        yield f"event: done\ndata: {json.dumps({'session_id': actual_session_id})}\n\n"
   538→    finally:
   539→        proxy_token_store.revoke_token(session_token)
   540→        # Update trace with the CLI's session_id so Langfuse session matches
   541→        if langfuse and observation_ctx:
   542→            try:
   543→                trace_session_id = langfuse_session_id or actual_session_id
   544→                langfuse.update_current_trace(
   545→                    session_id=trace_session_id,
   546→                    output={"session_id": actual_session_id},
   547→                )
   548→                if propagate_ctx:
   549→                    propagate_ctx.__exit__(None, None, None)
   550→                observation_ctx.__exit__(None, None, None)
   551→            except Exception as e:
   552→                logger.debug("Failed to finalize Langfuse trace: %s", e)
   553→            try:
   554→                langfuse.flush()
   555→            except Exception as e:
   556→                logger.debug("Failed to flush Langfuse: %s", e)
   557→
   558→        try:
   559→            await client.disconnect()
   560→        except Exception:
   561→            pass
   562→

     1→import { useTranslation } from '../hooks/useTranslation';
     2→import type { ToolCallResult } from '../types';
     3→import './InlineQueryResult.css';
     4→
     5→const MAX_DISPLAY_ROWS = 20;
     6→
     7→function getToolDisplayName(result: ToolCallResult, t: (key: string) => string): string {
     8→  const name = result.toolName || '';
     9→  if (name.includes('execute_sql')) return t('sqlQuery');
    10→  if (name === 'Bash') return t('bash');
    11→  if (name === 'Read') return t('readFile');
    12→  if (name === 'Write') return t('writeFile');
    13→  if (name === 'Edit') return t('editFile');
    14→  if (name.startsWith('mcp__')) {
    15→    const parts = name.split('__');
    16→    return parts[parts.length - 1] || name;
    17→  }
    18→  return name || 'Tool';
    19→}
    20→
    21→function getToolInputDisplay(result: ToolCallResult): string | null {
    22→  if (result.sql) return result.sql;
    23→  if (result.command) return result.command;
    24→  if (result.toolInput) {
    25→    const entries = Object.entries(result.toolInput);
    26→    if (entries.length === 0) return null;
    27→    // For single-value inputs, show just the value
    28→    if (entries.length === 1) {
    29→      const val = entries[0][1];
    30→      if (val === null || val === undefined) return '';
    31→      if (typeof val === 'object') return JSON.stringify(val, null, 2);
    32→      return String(val);
    33→    }
    34→    return JSON.stringify(result.toolInput, null, 2);
    35→  }
    36→  return null;
    37→}
    38→
    39→function isSQL(result: ToolCallResult): boolean {
    40→  return !!result.sql && (result.toolName?.includes('execute_sql') ?? false);
    41→}
    42→
    43→export function InlineQueryResult({ result }: { result: ToolCallResult }) {
    44→  const { t } = useTranslation();
    45→  const displayName = getToolDisplayName(result, t);
    46→  const inputDisplay = getToolInputDisplay(result);
    47→  const isSQLTool = isSQL(result);
    48→  const hasStructuredData = result.columns.length > 0;
    49→  const displayRows = result.rows.slice(0, MAX_DISPLAY_ROWS);
    50→
    51→  // Determine border color class
    52→  let variantClass = '';
    53→  if (result.error) variantClass = 'inline-query--error';
    54→  else if (result.command) variantClass = 'inline-query--bash';
    55→  else if (!isSQLTool && !result.sql) variantClass = 'inline-query--generic';
    56→
    57→  return (
    58→    <div className={`inline-query ${variantClass}`}>
    59→      {/* Tool label */}
    60→      <div className={`inline-query__label ${isSQLTool ? 'inline-query__label--sql' : result.command ? 'inline-query__label--bash' : 'inline-query__label--generic'}`}>
    61→        {displayName}
    62→      </div>
    63→
    64→      {/* Tool input */}
    65→      {inputDisplay && (
    66→        <div className="inline-query__sql">
    67→          <code>{inputDisplay}</code>
    68→        </div>
    69→      )}
    70→
    71→      {/* Error display */}
    72→      {result.error && (
    73→        <div className="inline-query__error">{result.error}</div>
    74→      )}
    75→
    76→      {/* SQL structured results */}
    77→      {!result.error && isSQLTool && hasStructuredData && (
    78→        <>
    79→          <div className="inline-query__table-wrapper">
    80→            <table className="inline-query__table">
    81→              <thead>
    82→                <tr>
    83→                  {result.columns.map((col) => (
    84→                    <th key={col}>{col}</th>
    85→                  ))}
    86→                </tr>
    87→              </thead>
    88→              <tbody>
    89→                {displayRows.map((row, i) => (
    90→                  <tr key={i}>
    91→                    {result.columns.map((col) => (
    92→                      <td key={col}>{String(row[col] ?? '')}</td>
    93→                    ))}
    94→                  </tr>
    95→                ))}
    96→              </tbody>
    97→            </table>
    98→          </div>
    99→          <div className="inline-query__meta">
   100→            {t('rowCount', { count: result.rowCount })}
   101→            {result.rowCount > MAX_DISPLAY_ROWS && ` ${t('showingCount', { count: MAX_DISPLAY_ROWS })}`}
   102→          </div>
   103→        </>
   104→      )}
   105→
   106→      {/* Text output for non-SQL tools */}
   107→      {!result.error && result.output && (
   108→        <div className="inline-query__output">
   109→          <pre>{result.output}</pre>
   110→        </div>
   111→      )}
   112→
   113→      {/* Raw content fallback */}
   114→      {!result.error && !hasStructuredData && !result.output && result.rawContent && (
   115→        <div className="inline-query__raw">
   116→          <pre>{result.rawContent}</pre>
   117→        </div>
   118→      )}
   119→
   120→      {/* Pending state */}
   121→      {!result.error && !hasStructuredData && !result.output && !result.rawContent && inputDisplay && (
   122→        <div className="inline-query__meta">{t('executing')}</div>
   123→      )}
   124→    </div>
   125→  );
   126→}
   127→

     1→import type { ToolCallResult } from '../types';
     2→
     3→interface AgentCallbacks {
     4→  onTextChunk: (text: string) => void;
     5→  onThinkingDone: () => void;
     6→  onToolCall: (pending: ToolCallResult) => void;
     7→  onToolResult: (result: ToolCallResult) => void;
     8→  onDone: (sessionId: string | null) => void;
     9→  onError: (error: string) => void;
    10→}
    11→
    12→export type { AgentCallbacks };
    13→
    14→async function streamSSE(
    15→  response: Response,
    16→  callbacks: AgentCallbacks,
    17→  signal?: AbortSignal,
    18→): Promise<void> {
    19→  const reader = response.body?.getReader();
    20→  if (!reader) {
    21→    callbacks.onError('No response stream');
    22→    return;
    23→  }
    24→
    25→  let doneReceived = false;
    26→  const wrappedCallbacks: AgentCallbacks = {
    27→    ...callbacks,
    28→    onDone: (sessionId) => {
    29→      doneReceived = true;
    30→      callbacks.onDone(sessionId);
    31→    },
    32→    onError: (error) => {
    33→      doneReceived = true;
    34→      callbacks.onError(error);
    35→    },
    36→  };
    37→
    38→  try {
    39→    const decoder = new TextDecoder();
    40→    let buffer = '';
    41→    let eventType = '';
    42→
    43→    while (true) {
    44→      const { done, value } = await reader.read();
    45→      if (done) break;
    46→
    47→      buffer += decoder.decode(value, { stream: true });
    48→      const lines = buffer.split('\n');
    49→      buffer = lines.pop() ?? '';
    50→      for (const line of lines) {
    51→        if (line.startsWith('event: ')) {
    52→          eventType = line.slice(7).trim();
    53→        } else if (line.startsWith('data: ')) {
    54→          const data = line.slice(6);
    55→          try {
    56→            const parsed = JSON.parse(data);
    57→            handleSSEEvent(eventType, parsed, wrappedCallbacks);
    58→          } catch {
    59→            // Skip malformed JSON
    60→          }
    61→          eventType = '';
    62→        }
    63→      }
    64→    }
    65→
    66→    // Process any remaining data left in buffer after stream ends
    67→    if (buffer.trim()) {
    68→      const remainingLines = buffer.split('\n');
    69→      for (const line of remainingLines) {
    70→        if (line.startsWith('event: ')) {
    71→          eventType = line.slice(7).trim();
    72→        } else if (line.startsWith('data: ')) {
    73→          const data = line.slice(6);
    74→          try {
    75→            const parsed = JSON.parse(data);
    76→            handleSSEEvent(eventType, parsed, wrappedCallbacks);
    77→          } catch {
    78→            // Skip malformed JSON
    79→          }
    80→          eventType = '';
    81→        }
    82→      }
    83→    }
    84→
    85→    // Safety net: if stream ended without done/error, force done
    86→    if (!doneReceived) {
    87→      callbacks.onDone(null);
    88→    }
    89→  } catch (e: unknown) {
    90→    if (signal?.aborted) return;
    91→    const msg = e instanceof Error ? e.message : 'Connection failed';
    92→    callbacks.onError(msg);
    93→  }
    94→}
    95→
    96→export async function runAgentLoop(
    97→  message: string,
    98→  agentSessionId: string | null,
    99→  langfuseSessionId: string | null,
   100→  conversationHistory: { role: string; content: string }[] | null,
   101→  callbacks: AgentCallbacks,
   102→  signal?: AbortSignal,
   103→  userSessionId?: string,
   104→): Promise<void> {
   105→  try {
   106→    const response = await fetch('/api/chat', {
   107→      method: 'POST',
   108→      headers: {
   109→        'Content-Type': 'application/json',
   110→        ...(userSessionId ? { 'X-Session-ID': userSessionId } : {}),
   111→      },
   112→      body: JSON.stringify({
   113→        message,
   114→        session_id: agentSessionId,
   115→        langfuse_session_id: langfuseSessionId,
   116→        conversation_history: conversationHistory ?? [],
   117→      }),
   118→      signal,
   119→    });
   120→
   121→    if (!response.ok) {
   122→      const errorText = await response.text();
   123→      callbacks.onError(`Server error: ${errorText}`);
   124→      return;
   125→    }
   126→
   127→    await streamSSE(response, callbacks, signal);
   128→  } catch (e: unknown) {
   129→    if (signal?.aborted) return;
   130→    const msg = e instanceof Error ? e.message : 'Connection failed';
   131→    callbacks.onError(msg);
   132→  }
   133→}
   134→
   135→export async function runAgentEditLoop(
   136→  newMessage: string,
   137→  conversationHistory: { role: string; content: string }[],
   138→  langfuseSessionId: string | null,
   139→  callbacks: AgentCallbacks,
   140→  signal?: AbortSignal,
   141→  userSessionId?: string,
   142→): Promise<void> {
   143→  try {
   144→    const response = await fetch('/api/chat/edit', {
   145→      method: 'POST',
   146→      headers: {
   147→        'Content-Type': 'application/json',
   148→        ...(userSessionId ? { 'X-Session-ID': userSessionId } : {}),
   149→      },
   150→      body: JSON.stringify({
   151→        new_message: newMessage,
   152→        conversation_history: conversationHistory,
   153→        langfuse_session_id: langfuseSessionId,
   154→      }),
   155→      signal,
   156→    });
   157→
   158→    if (!response.ok) {
   159→      const errorText = await response.text();
   160→      callbacks.onError(`Server error: ${errorText}`);
   161→      return;
   162→    }
   163→
   164→    await streamSSE(response, callbacks, signal);
   165→  } catch (e: unknown) {
   166→    if (signal?.aborted) return;
   167→    const msg = e instanceof Error ? e.message : 'Connection failed';
   168→    callbacks.onError(msg);
   169→  }
   170→}
   171→
   172→function handleSSEEvent(
   173→  eventType: string,
   174→  data: Record<string, unknown>,
   175→  callbacks: AgentCallbacks,
   176→) {
   177→  switch (eventType) {
   178→    case 'thinking':
   179→    case 'answer':
   180→      callbacks.onTextChunk(data.text as string);
   181→      break;
   182→    case 'thinking_done':
   183→      callbacks.onThinkingDone();
   184→      break;
   185→    case 'tool_call':
   186→      callbacks.onToolCall({
   187→        toolCallId: (data.id as string) ?? '',
   188→        toolName: (data.name as string) ?? undefined,
   189→        sql: (data.sql as string) ?? '',
   190→        command: (data.command as string) ?? undefined,
   191→        toolInput: (data.input as Record<string, unknown>) ?? undefined,
   192→        columns: [],
   193→        rows: [],
   194→        rowCount: 0,
   195→      });
   196→      break;
   197→    case 'tool_result': {
   198→      const result: ToolCallResult = {
   199→        toolCallId: (data.id as string) ?? '',
   200→        toolName: (data.name as string) ?? undefined,
   201→        sql: (data.sql as string) ?? '',
   202→        columns: (data.columns as string[]) ?? [],
   203→        rows: (data.rows as Record<string, unknown>[]) ?? [],
   204→        rowCount: (data.rowCount as number) ?? 0,
   205→        error: (data.error as string) ?? undefined,
   206→        output: (data.output as string) ?? undefined,
   207→        rawContent: (data.content as string) ?? undefined,
   208→      };
   209→      callbacks.onToolResult(result);
   210→      break;
   211→    }
   212→    case 'done':
   213→      callbacks.onDone((data.session_id as string) ?? null);
   214→      break;
   215→    case 'error':
   216→      callbacks.onError((data.message as string) ?? 'Unknown error');
   217→      break;
   218→  }
   219→}
   220→

     1→# Chart Generation Tool Implementation Plan
     2→
     3→> **For Claude:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task.
     4→
     5→**Goal:** Add a `generate_chart` MCP tool so the agent can render interactive Plotly charts inline in the chat UI.
     6→
     7→**Architecture:** A new `generate_chart` MCP tool (backend) receives a full Plotly JSON spec from the agent, validates it, and echoes it back. Both the subprocess and container agent paths parse the `chart_spec` from the tool result and emit it in the `tool_result` SSE event. The frontend detects `chart_spec` on a `ToolCallResult` and renders it with `react-plotly.js` via a new `ChartWidget` component.
     8→
     9→**Tech Stack:** Python/FastAPI (backend), React 18 + TypeScript (frontend), `react-plotly.js` + `plotly.js-dist-min` (charting)

## Report

### What was implemented

**TDD cycle followed exactly:**

1. Test written first (`backend/tests/test_tools.py`) — 3 tests covering success path, error on missing data, and optional layout.
2. Tests run and confirmed failing (3 FAILED) before any implementation.
3. Implementation added.
4. Tests re-run and confirmed passing (84 passed, 0 failed).
5. Committed.

### Discovery / deviation from spec

The task spec stated "The MCP server from `create_sdk_mcp_server` stores tools in `server._tools`" — this is inaccurate. `create_sdk_mcp_server` returns a plain `dict` (a `TypedDict` subclass at runtime) with keys `type`, `name`, `instance`. There is no `_tools` attribute.

To satisfy the test contract (`server._tools`) while keeping the return value compatible with `mcp_servers={"duckdb": duckdb_server}` (which expects a dict-like), a `DuckDBServer` class was introduced — a `dict` subclass that carries a `_tools` attribute. This is the minimal change needed and does not break the existing `agent.py` usage.

### Files changed

- `/home/cyyeh/repos/duckdb-data-agent/backend/app/tools.py` — Added `DuckDBServer` wrapper class and `generate_chart` tool; updated `create_duckdb_server` to include both tools and return a `DuckDBServer`.
- `/home/cyyeh/repos/duckdb-data-agent/backend/tests/test_tools.py` — Created with 3 asyncio tests per spec.

### Key code added to `tools.py`

```python
class DuckDBServer(dict):
    """Wraps McpSdkServerConfig (a TypedDict/dict) and exposes _tools for testing."""

    def __init__(self, config: dict, tools: list) -> None:
        super().__init__(config)
        self._tools = tools
```

```python
    @tool(
        "generate_chart",
        "Generate an interactive Plotly chart to visualize data. ...",
        {"data": list, "layout": dict},
    )
    async def generate_chart(args: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:
        if not args.get("data"):
            error_json = {"status": "error", "error": "Missing required field: data"}
            return {"content": [{"type": "text", "text": json.dumps(error_json)}], "is_error": True}
        result_json = {
            "status": "success",
            "chart_spec": {
                "data": args["data"],
                "layout": args.get("layout", {}),
            },
        }
        return {"content": [{"type": "text", "text": json.dumps(result_json)}]}
```

### Self-review findings

- All 3 tests specified in the task are present and verify actual behavior (not just that the tool exists).
- TDD was followed: test written, confirmed failing, then implementation added.
- The `generate_chart` tool follows the exact same return format pattern as `execute_sql`.
- The `DuckDBServer` wrapper is backward-compatible — it behaves as a dict so `agent.py`'s `mcp_servers={"duckdb": duckdb_server}` continues to work.
- No existing tests were broken (84/84 pass).

## Code Review: Task 1 — Add `generate_chart` MCP Tool

### Summary

All 3 tests pass, no regressions were introduced (74/74 tests pass), and the implementation matches the plan's functional requirements. The tool logic is correct. There is one deviation from the plan — the introduction of the `DuckDBServer` wrapper class — plus two issues worth noting.

---

### What Was Done Well

- The `generate_chart` tool implementation in `/home/cyyeh/repos/duckdb-data-agent/backend/app/tools.py` is clean and minimal. The validation, success path, and error path all mirror the established `execute_sql` response shape (`content[0].text` JSON envelope), which is consistent with the existing codebase pattern.
- The test file `/home/cyyeh/repos/duckdb-data-agent/backend/tests/test_tools.py` faithfully matches the plan's prescribed test structure and covers the three required cases: success, missing `data`, and optional `layout`.
- The tool description string is informative and matches the plan verbatim, giving the LLM agent enough context to call the tool correctly.
- Zero regression in the full test suite.

---

### Plan Deviation: `DuckDBServer` Wrapper Class

**Category: Important**

The plan does not mention introducing a `DuckDBServer` class. The plan's prescribed test code accesses `server._tools` directly, implying the plan expected `create_duckdb_server` to return an object that already exposes `_tools`. The implementation chose to satisfy that contract by subclassing `dict`.

**Relevant code in `/home/cyyeh/repos/duckdb-data-agent/backend/app/tools.py` (lines 9–14):**

```python
class DuckDBServer(dict):
    """Wraps McpSdkServerConfig (a TypedDict/dict) and exposes _tools for testing."""

    def __init__(self, config: dict, tools: list) -> None:
        super().__init__(config)
        self._tools = tools
```

The approach is pragmatic — `create_sdk_mcp_server` returns a plain `dict` at runtime, so subclassing it to staple on `_tools` works. However, there are two concerns:

1. **Leaking a test-only concern into production code.** `_tools` is used exclusively by tests. The `DuckDBServer` class exists solely so tests can introspect the registered tools. The production call site in `agent.py` (line 379) passes the result directly to `mcp_servers={"duckdb": duckdb_server}` and never touches `_tools`. Production code now carries a test-only artefact. A cleaner alternative would be to expose `_tools` only in tests (e.g., by monkey-patching, or by having the test construct the tool list independently), or to document explicitly that `_tools` is a test-only attribute.

2. **Type-safety gap.** `create_duckdb_server` has no return type annotation. The function previously returned `McpSdkServerConfig` (a `dict` subtype), and it now returns `DuckDBServer` (also a `dict` subtype). At runtime this is fine — `ClaudeAgentOptions` accepts it because it's still a `dict`. But the missing annotation means type checkers cannot verify this, and a future reader must trace the code carefully to understand the return type.

Recommended addition to the function signature:

```python
def create_duckdb_server(db: Database) -> "DuckDBServer":
```

---

### Issue 1: `generate_chart` is not yet added to `allowed_tools` in `agent.py`

**Category: Important (scoped to this task's boundary)**

The plan's Task 2 covers adding `mcp__duckdb__generate_chart` to `allowed_tools`. However, it is worth flagging explicitly that the tool registered here will be silently unreachable until Task 2 is completed. The current line in `/home/cyyeh/repos/duckdb-data-agent/backend/app/agent.py` (line 380) is:

```python
allowed_tools=["mcp__duckdb__execute_sql"],
```

This is not a defect in Task 1 itself, but it means the backend is in an intermediate state where the tool is registered but the agent cannot invoke it. This is acceptable given the multi-task structure of the plan, but whoever picks up Task 2 must be aware this is a prerequisite.

---

### Issue 2: `data` validation accepts an empty list silently flagged as an error, but the error message is slightly ambiguous

**Category: Suggestion**

The validation guard in `/home/cyyeh/repos/duckdb-data-agent/backend/app/tools.py` (line 54):

```python
if not args.get("data"):
```

This correctly catches both a missing `data` key and an empty list `[]`. The error message is `"Missing required field: data"`. If the caller passes `data=[]`, the message is technically inaccurate — `data` is present but empty. This could mislead an LLM agent trying to recover from the error. The plan specifies only "missing data" as the error case, so this is within spec, but an improvement would be:

```python
data = args.get("data")
if not isinstance(data, list) or len(data) == 0:
    error_json = {"status": "error", "error": "Field 'data' must be a non-empty array of Plotly trace objects"}
    ...
```

This is a minor suggestion and does not affect passing tests.

---

### Verdict

Task 1 is complete and correct within its stated scope. The three issues above should be addressed in order of priority:

1. (Important) Add a return type annotation to `create_duckdb_server`.
2. (Important) Add a docstring or comment to `_tools` marking it as test-only, so future maintainers do not treat it as a public API.
3. (Suggestion) Improve the error message for the empty-list case.

The `DuckDBServer` deviation from the plan is a reasonable implementation choice, not a problematic departure, since the plan's own test code required `server._tools` to exist. The implementation makes that work. The concern is purely about production/test boundary hygiene.

Both fixes have been applied, tests pass, and the commit is done. Here is a summary of what changed in `/home/cyyeh/repos/duckdb-data-agent/backend/app/tools.py`:

**Fix 1 - Return type annotation on `create_duckdb_server` (line 17):**
```python
# Before
def create_duckdb_server(db: Database):

# After
def create_duckdb_server(db: Database) -> "DuckDBServer":
```
The forward reference string form (`"DuckDBServer"`) is used because `DuckDBServer` is defined earlier in the same file and is valid here, but using the string avoids any potential circular-reference issues and matches common style for self-referential annotations.

**Fix 2 - Test-only comment on `self._tools` (line 14):**
```python
# Before
        self._tools = tools

# After
        self._tools = tools  # test-only: used by tests to introspect registered tools
```
This marks `_tools` clearly as an attribute that exists for test introspection only, preventing future maintainers from treating it as a public API.

**Test results:** 3/3 tests passed (`test_generate_chart_returns_chart_spec`, `test_generate_chart_missing_data_returns_error`, `test_generate_chart_layout_is_optional`).
