## Implementation Report

### What Was Implemented

Four changes were applied to `/home/cyyeh/repos/duckdb-data-agent/backend/app/agent.py`, plus one new test file created.

**1. System prompt update (`build_system_prompt`, lines 52–73)**

A new `## Chart Generation` section was appended to the prompt string. It instructs the agent to call the `generate_chart` tool after SQL queries when a chart would help, and specifies the expected Plotly figure spec format (`data` array of trace objects, optional `layout` object).

**2. `allowed_tools` expansion (subprocess path, `stream_chat`)**

Changed from:
```python
allowed_tools=["mcp__duckdb__execute_sql"],
```
To:
```python
allowed_tools=["mcp__duckdb__execute_sql", "mcp__duckdb__generate_chart"],
```

**3. Subprocess `UserMessage` handler — `chart_spec` parsing**

The handler previously set `result_data["output"] = output` unconditionally for non-SQL tool results. It now attempts to parse the output as JSON and, when the parsed object has `status == "success"` and a `"chart_spec"` key, stores the chart spec under `result_data["chart_spec"]` instead of `result_data["output"]`. Error paths remain unchanged.

**4. Container `user` message handler — `chart_spec` parsing**

The existing JSON parsing block that populated `columns`/`rows`/`rowCount` on success now checks for `"chart_spec"` in the parsed object first. If present, it populates `result_data["chart_spec"]` instead of the tabular fields. SQL results (which never contain `chart_spec`) are unaffected.

### New Test File

`/home/cyyeh/repos/duckdb-data-agent/backend/tests/test_agent_chart.py` — one test:
- `test_build_system_prompt_mentions_generate_chart`: verifies `"generate_chart"` and `"chart"` both appear in the built prompt.

### Test Results

- Target tests (`test_agent_chart.py`, `test_tools.py`): **4/4 passed**
- Full suite (75 tests, excluding container tests): **75/75 passed** — no regressions

### Self-Review Findings

- The subprocess handler now omits `output` from `result_data` when JSON parsing succeeds but no `chart_spec` is present (e.g., a future tool returning `{"status": "success", "data": ...}`). In that case it falls back to `result_data["output"] = output`, which is correct.
- The container handler correctly preserves the SQL result path: since `execute_sql` results contain `columns`/`rows`/`rowCount` and never `chart_spec`, the added `if "chart_spec" in parsed` branch is transparent to existing behaviour.
- The `allowed_tools` change is only in the subprocess path; the container path does not use that list (it's controlled by the sidecar's own configuration), so no change is needed there.

## Code Review: Task 2 — Update Agent for `generate_chart` Support

### Summary

The implementation covers all five deliverables described in Task 2: system prompt update, `allowed_tools` expansion, subprocess `UserMessage` handler, container `user` message handler, and the test file. All 75 backend tests pass with no regressions. The diff is clean and minimal.

---

### Plan Alignment

The plan specifies four file changes and one new test file. The diff touches exactly those files:

- `backend/app/agent.py` — all four targeted regions modified
- `backend/tests/test_agent_chart.py` — created as specified

No unintended files were modified.

---

### What Was Done Well

1. The system prompt section is faithful to the plan's wording and includes all required detail: trace types, the `layout` field description, and the proactive-use instruction.
2. The `allowed_tools` change is a precise one-line update with no scope creep.
3. Both execution paths (subprocess via `UserMessage`/`ToolResultBlock` at line 509, and container via raw JSON parsing at line 294) handle the `chart_spec` case, maintaining behavioural parity between them.
4. The `try/except (json.JSONDecodeError, AttributeError)` fallback is appropriate and mirrors existing error handling patterns in the file.
5. The test file imports `_extract_tool_result_text` even though it is not used in any assertion — this is harmless but worth noting (see below).

---

### Issues Found

#### Important

**1. Behavioural asymmetry between the two paths when `generate_chart` returns an error**

File: `/home/cyyeh/repos/duckdb-data-agent/backend/app/agent.py`

In the subprocess path (line 523):
```python
if block.is_error:
    result_data["error"] = output
else:
    # JSON parse attempt for chart_spec
```

In the container path (lines 303–310):
```python
elif parsed.get("status") == "error":
    result_data["error"] = parsed.get("error", "")
...
if block.get("is_error"):
    result_data["error"] = text
```

The container path checks `is_error` _after_ the JSON-parse block (line 309), which means a successful-status JSON response that also carries `is_error: true` at the block level will have `result_data["chart_spec"]` set and then `result_data["error"]` set on top of it — leaving both keys present simultaneously. The subprocess path avoids this because it checks `block.is_error` first and skips the JSON-parse entirely. This discrepancy was present before Task 2 for the SQL path and was not introduced here, but now that `generate_chart` errors are a realistic code path, it becomes more observable. This is a pre-existing bug that Task 2 did not worsen, but it is now more reachable.

**2. The test file tests only the happy-path system prompt assertion**

File: `/home/cyyeh/repos/duckdb-data-agent/backend/tests/test_agent_chart.py`

The plan specified only one test for Task 2 (the system prompt check), and the implementation delivers exactly that. However, the plan's reasoning for the minimal test scope is that the more important parsing logic is tested indirectly through the tools tests. Given that the subprocess and container parsing paths both have non-trivial branching (error detection, JSON fallback, `chart_spec` vs. `output` routing), there are no unit tests exercising `_extract_tool_result_text` or the `chart_spec`-parsing branch in the `UserMessage` handler directly. The import of `_extract_tool_result_text` in the test file at line 2 suggests a test for it was anticipated but not written.

This leaves a gap: a regression in the subprocess `UserMessage` handler's `chart_spec` branch would not be caught by any test. This is within the plan's scope to flag since the plan explicitly calls out `_extract_tool_result_text` in the import.

---

#### Suggestions

**3. Unused import in the test file**

File: `/home/cyyeh/repos/duckdb-data-agent/backend/tests/test_agent_chart.py`, line 2:

```python
from app.agent import build_system_prompt, _extract_tool_result_text
```

`_extract_tool_result_text` is imported but never referenced in any test assertion. This will not cause a failure, but it is dead code in the test file and suggests the intent was to write at least one more test. It should either be removed or used in an additional test.

**4. Non-SQL tool output on error does not preserve structured error message in subprocess path**

File: `/home/cyyeh/repos/duckdb-data-agent/backend/app/agent.py`, lines 523–524:

```python
if block.is_error:
    result_data["error"] = output
```

When `generate_chart` returns `{"status": "error", "error": "Missing required field: data"}` and the MCP layer marks `is_error: True`, the raw JSON string (not the inner `"error"` field) is stored in `result_data["error"]`. The container path (line 304) correctly extracts `parsed.get("error", "")`. This means the frontend will receive a raw JSON string as the error message in the subprocess path but a clean human-readable string in the container path. Not a critical issue at this stage since errors are displayed, but it may produce a worse UX.

### Changes Made

**`/home/cyyeh/repos/duckdb-data-agent/backend/tests/test_agent_chart.py` — line 2**

Removed the unused `_extract_tool_result_text` import:
```python
# Before
from app.agent import build_system_prompt, _extract_tool_result_text

# After
from app.agent import build_system_prompt
```

**`/home/cyyeh/repos/duckdb-data-agent/backend/app/agent.py` — `UserMessage` handler, around line 523**

Replaced the raw assignment of the full JSON string to `result_data["error"]` with a try/except block that parses the JSON and extracts only the inner `"error"` field:
```python
# Before
if block.is_error:
    result_data["error"] = output

# After
if block.is_error:
    try:
        parsed_error = json.loads(output)
        result_data["error"] = parsed_error.get("error", output)
    except (json.JSONDecodeError, AttributeError):
        result_data["error"] = output
```

This ensures that when a tool like `generate_chart` returns a JSON error response such as `{"status": "error", "error": "Missing required field: data"}`, the `result_data["error"]` field receives the clean message `"Missing required field: data"` rather than the entire JSON string.

## Code Review: Tasks 3 and 4 - Frontend Charting Dependencies and TypeScript Types

### Summary

Both tasks are correctly implemented, fully aligned with the plan, and the TypeScript compiler confirms there are no type errors. The work is clean and minimal. However, there is one important issue worth flagging and one suggestion.

---

### What Was Done Well

**Task 3 - Dependency Installation:**
- All three packages specified in the plan are present and correctly categorized:
  - `react-plotly.js@2.6.0` and `plotly.js-dist-min@3.4.0` in `dependencies` (runtime)
  - `@types/react-plotly.js@2.6.4` in `devDependencies` (compile-time only)
- Both `package.json` and `package-lock.json` were committed together, which is correct practice.
- Versions are pinned with `^` semver ranges, consistent with the existing codebase convention.
- The commit message matches the plan exactly: `feat: add react-plotly.js and plotly.js-dist-min dependencies`.

**Task 4 - TypeScript Type:**
- The `chart_spec` field was added exactly as specified in the plan (lines 32-35 of `/home/cyyeh/repos/duckdb-data-agent/frontend/src/types.ts`):
  ```typescript
  chart_spec?: {
    data: unknown[];
    layout?: Record<string, unknown>;
  };
  ```
- Field is optional (`?`), correctly placed after `rawContent`, and matches the shape the backend produces.
- `npx tsc --noEmit` passes with zero errors.
- The commit is atomic - only `types.ts` was modified.

---

### Important Issue: Redundant `plotly.js` Full Bundle in `node_modules`

**File:** `/home/cyyeh/repos/duckdb-data-agent/frontend/node_modules/react-plotly.js/react-plotly.js`

**Observation:** The default `react-plotly.js` export hard-codes a require path to `plotly.js/dist/plotly`:

```javascript
var _plotly = _interopRequireDefault(require("plotly.js/dist/plotly"));
```

This means that even though `plotly.js-dist-min` was installed as an explicit dependency, npm also resolved the full `plotly.js` bundle as a transitive dependency (because `react-plotly.js` declares `"plotly.js": ">1.34.0"` as a peer dependency, and npm resolved it). Both are present and both are 4.7 MB minified:

```
node_modules/plotly.js           (version 3.4.0, full bundle - pulled in automatically)
node_modules/plotly.js-dist-min  (version 3.4.0, minified distribution bundle)
```

At runtime (when the Vite bundler processes the code), the default `import Plot from 'react-plotly.js'` will bundle the **full** `plotly.js` source, not the smaller `plotly.js-dist-min`. The `plotly.js-dist-min` package is present but **not actually used** unless the code explicitly imports via the factory pattern:

```typescript
// This pattern (not yet implemented) would use plotly.js-dist-min:
import createPlotlyComponent from 'react-plotly.js/factory';
import Plotly from 'plotly.js-dist-min';
const Plot = createPlotlyComponent(Plotly);
```

This is not a blocker for the current tasks (Task 3 and 4 are purely about installation and types), but it will affect Task 6 (`ChartWidget`). The plan's stated goal of using `plotly.js-dist-min` will only be realized if Task 6 uses the factory pattern rather than the default import.

The agent implementing Task 6 should be made aware of this before starting that task.

---

### Suggestion: Inline Type vs Named Interface for `chart_spec`

**File:** `/home/cyyeh/repos/duckdb-data-agent/frontend/src/types.ts`

The `chart_spec` shape is defined inline within `ToolCallResult`:

```typescript
chart_spec?: {
  data: unknown[];
  layout?: Record<string, unknown>;
};
```

This shape will be referenced again in Task 5 (`agentService.ts` cast), Task 6 (`ChartWidget` props), and Task 7 (`InlineQueryResult` usage). Defining it as a named exported interface would avoid redundancy:

```typescript
export interface ChartSpec {
  data: unknown[];
  layout?: Record<string, unknown>;
}
```

This is not required by the plan and is a minor forward-looking concern. The current implementation matches the plan exactly and is not wrong. This is worth considering before Task 6 is implemented.

---

|---|---|
| `react-plotly.js` installed as runtime dependency | Satisfied |
| `plotly.js-dist-min` installed as runtime dependency | Satisfied (installed but see important issue above) |
| `@types/react-plotly.js` installed as devDependency | Satisfied |
| `chart_spec` field added to `ToolCallResult` | Satisfied |
| `chart_spec.data` typed as `unknown[]` | Satisfied |
| `chart_spec.layout` optional, typed as `Record<string, unknown>` | Satisfied |
| `npx tsc --noEmit` passes | Satisfied |
| Both `package.json` and `package-lock.json` committed | Satisfied |

**Overall: Both tasks are correctly implemented and ready to proceed to Task 5. Before Task 6 (`ChartWidget`) is started, the implementing agent should be informed that the default `import Plot from 'react-plotly.js'` will bundle the full `plotly.js` source rather than `plotly.js-dist-min`, and should use the factory pattern to wire up `plotly.js-dist-min` explicitly.**

---

## Code Review: Chart Generation Feature

### Overall Assessment

The implementation is solid and delivers what was planned. All 10 files from the plan's summary are present, all 75 backend tests pass with zero regressions, TypeScript compiles cleanly, and the core data path (tool -> SSE -> frontend render) is correctly wired end to end. The deviations from the plan are either improvements or inconsequential. There are a small number of issues worth addressing.

---

### What Was Done Well

- **Tool design is correct and minimal.** `generate_chart` is a pure echo tool — it validates the spec and returns it, keeping rendering logic entirely in the frontend. This is the right separation of concerns.
- **Both agent paths are covered.** The subprocess path (`UserMessage` handler) and the container path (`user` message JSON block) both parse `chart_spec` consistently.
- **Factory pattern in ChartWidget is the right call.** The plan specified `import Plot from 'react-plotly.js'` (direct default import), but the implementation uses `createPlotlyComponent(Plotly)` with `plotly.js-dist-min`. This is a well-known improvement: it avoids bundling the full 3MB `plotly.js` and lets Vite tree-shake the minified distribution build. This is a justified deviation that improves bundle size.
- **Error path in subprocess `UserMessage` handler is an improvement over the plan.** The plan specified `result_data["error"] = output` (raw string) when `block.is_error`. The implementation instead parses the JSON first and extracts `parsed.get("error", output)`, which means the UI sees a clean error message rather than a raw JSON string. This is a meaningful improvement in UX.
- **Test coverage matches plan exactly** for `test_tools.py` (3 tests) and `test_agent_chart.py` (1 test). All pass.

---

### Issues Found

#### Important (Should Fix)

**1. Unused `import json` in `test_agent_chart.py`**

File: `/home/cyyeh/repos/duckdb-data-agent/backend/tests/test_agent_chart.py`, line 1.

The plan's test file template imported `_extract_tool_result_text` alongside `json`, anticipating that additional tests would use them. The implementation correctly dropped the unused `_extract_tool_result_text` import but left behind `import json`, which is never referenced in the file.

```python
# Current - line 1 is dead code
import json
from app.agent import build_system_prompt
```

This is not harmful but is dead code that will cause a linter warning. Remove `import json`.

**2. Missing CSS rule for `.inline-query--chart`**

File: `/home/cyyeh/repos/duckdb-data-agent/frontend/src/components/InlineQueryResult.css`

The chart branch in `InlineQueryResult.tsx` (line 55) applies the class `inline-query--chart` to the wrapper div:

```tsx
<div className="inline-query inline-query--chart">
```

However, `.inline-query--chart` has no corresponding CSS rule in `InlineQueryResult.css`. The other variant classes (`.inline-query--error`, `.inline-query--bash`, `.inline-query--generic`) all have rules that set a distinct `border-color`. The chart container will render with the default `border-color: var(--color-border-light)` from the base `.inline-query` rule — which works, but is inconsistent with how the other variants are styled and makes it impossible to visually distinguish or target chart blocks in the future.

A minimal rule should be added:

```css
.inline-query--chart {
  border-color: var(--color-border-light);
  overflow: visible; /* Plotly tooltips can extend beyond container bounds */
}
```

The `overflow: visible` point is especially worth noting: the base `.inline-query` rule sets `overflow: hidden` (line 5 of `InlineQueryResult.css`), which will clip Plotly's hover tooltips and zoom controls at the container boundary. Chart containers need `overflow: visible` to allow Plotly's interactive overlays to render correctly.

**3. Container path `is_error` override leaves raw JSON in the error field**

File: `/home/cyyeh/repos/duckdb-data-agent/backend/app/agent.py`, lines 309–310.

In the container path, the `is_error` check at the bottom of the tool result handler does not parse the JSON:

```python
if block.get("is_error"):
    result_data["error"] = text   # text is raw JSON string
```

This is the same pre-existing behavior from the base SHA — it was not changed in this PR. But the subprocess path was improved to parse the JSON error. The two paths are now inconsistent: in the subprocess path an error from `generate_chart` will show `Missing required field: data`; in the container path it will show `{"status": "error", "error": "Missing required field: data"}` as raw text in the UI.

This was a pre-existing issue, but since the subprocess path was corrected in this PR, the container path should be brought to parity. The fix is to mirror the subprocess logic:

```python
if block.get("is_error"):
    try:
        parsed_err = json.loads(text)
        result_data["error"] = parsed_err.get("error", text)
    except (json.JSONDecodeError, AttributeError):
        result_data["error"] = text
```

---

#### Suggestions (Nice to Have)

**4. No explicit `height` on `ChartWidget` — Plotly may render with zero height**

File: `/home/cyyeh/repos/duckdb-data-agent/frontend/src/components/ChartWidget.tsx`

The component sets `style={{ width: '100%' }}` and `useResizeHandler`, but does not set an explicit height. Plotly's default `height` in a layout is 450px when `autosize: true` is used without an explicit container height, so this will likely work in practice. However, if the parent container ever has `height: 0` (e.g., during initial render or in a flex context with no explicit height), the chart will not be visible until a resize event fires. A defensive default in the layout prop would make this more robust:

```tsx
layout={{ autosize: true, height: 400, ...layout } as Partial<Plotly.Layout>}
```

This also lets callers override `height` by passing it in the `layout` prop.

**5. `generate_chart` tool descriptor marks `layout` as required in the schema**

File: `/home/cyyeh/repos/duckdb-data-agent/backend/app/tools.py`, line 51.

```python
{"data": list, "layout": dict},
```

Both `data` and `layout` are listed in the tool schema dictionary with no indication that `layout` is optional. Depending on how `create_sdk_mcp_server` generates the JSON Schema from this dict, the agent may believe `layout` is required and always try to supply it (which is harmless but slightly increases token usage). The plan notes `layout` is optional. If the SDK supports marking fields optional (e.g., with `Optional[dict]` or `typing.Optional`), it is worth doing so.

**6. `test_agent_chart.py` does not test `allowed_tools`**

File: `/home/cyyeh/repos/duckdb-data-agent/backend/tests/test_agent_chart.py`

The plan's test file at Task 2, Step 1 included only the `build_system_prompt` test. The `allowed_tools` list update at line 390 of `agent.py` is not covered by any test. If `mcp__duckdb__generate_chart` were accidentally removed from `allowed_tools`, the agent would silently be unable to call the tool and no test would catch it. A test that verifies the tool name appears in `ClaudeAgentOptions.allowed_tools` would close this gap, though this is a minor concern given the simplicity of the change.

    10→function getLastThinkingLine(segments: ContentSegment[], streamingRemainder: string | undefined, t: (key: string) => string): string {
    11→  // Use streaming remainder if available
    12→  if (streamingRemainder?.trim()) {
    13→    const lines = streamingRemainder.trim().split('\n').filter((l) => l.trim());
    14→    const last = lines[lines.length - 1] || '';
    15→    return last.length > 100 ? last.slice(0, 100) + '...' : last;
    16→  }
    17→  // Otherwise use last thinking segment's last line
    18→  for (let i = segments.length - 1; i >= 0; i--) {
    19→    if (segments[i].type === 'thinking' && segments[i].text?.trim()) {
    20→      const lines = segments[i].text!.trim().split('\n').filter((l) => l.trim());

    22→      return last.length > 100 ? last.slice(0, 100) + '...' : last;

    24→  }

   209→              className="message-bubble__edit-btn message-bubble__edit-btn--save"
   210→              onClick={handleSaveEdit}
   211→            >
   212→              {t('saveResend')}
   213→            </button>
   214→            <button
   215→              className="message-bubble__edit-btn"
   216→              onClick={handleCancelEdit}
   217→            >
   218→              {t('cancel')}
   219→            </button>
   220→          </div>
   221→        </div>
   222→      ) : hasSegments ? (
   223→        <div className="message-bubble__segments">
   224→          <ThinkingBlock
   225→            segments={message.segments!}
   226→            streamingRemainder={isThinkingPhase ? streamingRemainder : undefined}
   227→            isThinkingPhase={isThinkingPhase}
   228→            isAgentStreaming={!!message.isStreaming}
   229→          />
   230→          {answerSegments.map((seg, i) => (
   231→            <div key={i} className="message-bubble__segment message-bubble__segment--answer">
   232→              <div className="message-bubble__segment-label message-bubble__segment-label--answer">{t('answer')}</div>
   233→              <div className="message-bubble__segment-content">
   234→                <ReactMarkdown remarkPlugins={[remarkGfm]}>{seg.text!}</ReactMarkdown>
   235→              </div>
   236→            </div>
   237→          ))}
   238→          {isInAnswerPhase && !hasAnswer && streamingRemainder?.trim() && (
   239→            <div className="message-bubble__segment message-bubble__segment--answer">
   240→              <div className="message-bubble__segment-label message-bubble__segment-label--answer">{t('answer')}</div>
   241→              <div className="message-bubble__segment-content">
   242→                <ReactMarkdown remarkPlugins={[remarkGfm]}>{streamingRemainder}</ReactMarkdown>
   243→              </div>
   244→            </div>
   245→          )}
   246→          {message.isStreaming && !message.content && (
   247→            <span className="message-bubble__typing">{t('thinking')}</span>
   248→          )}
   249→        </div>
   250→      ) : (
   251→        <>
   252→          <div className="message-bubble__content">
   253→            {message.content ? (
   254→              <ReactMarkdown remarkPlugins={[remarkGfm]}>{message.content}</ReactMarkdown>
   255→            ) : message.isStreaming ? (
   256→              <span className="message-bubble__typing">{t('thinking')}</span>
   257→            ) : null}
   258→          </div>
   259→          {message.toolCalls && message.toolCalls.length > 0 && (
   260→            <div className="message-bubble__tools">
   261→              {message.toolCalls.map((tc) => (
   262→                <InlineQueryResult key={tc.toolCallId} result={tc} />
   263→              ))}
   264→            </div>
   265→          )}
   266→        </>
   267→      )}
   268→    </div>
   269→  );
   270→}
   271→

     1→import {
     2→  useCallback,
     3→  useRef,
     4→  useState,
     5→  type ReactNode,
     6→} from 'react';
     7→import { AgentContext } from '../hooks/useAgent';
     8→import { runAgentLoop, runAgentEditLoop } from '../agent/agentService';
     9→import type { ChatMessage, ContentSegment, ToolCallResult } from '../types';
    10→import { useSessionId } from '../hooks/useSessionId';
    11→
    12→function generateId() {
    13→  return crypto.randomUUID();
    14→}
    15→
    16→export function AgentProvider({
    17→  children,
    18→  refreshTables,
    19→}: {
    20→  children: ReactNode;
    21→  refreshTables: () => Promise<void>;
    22→}) {
    23→  const userSessionId = useSessionId();
    24→  const [messages, setMessages] = useState<ChatMessage[]>([]);
    25→  const [isStreaming, setIsStreaming] = useState(false);
    26→  const abortRef = useRef<AbortController | null>(null);
    27→  const textBufferRef = useRef('');
    28→  const flushTimerRef = useRef<ReturnType<typeof setTimeout> | null>(null);
    29→  const assistantIdRef = useRef('');
    30→  const segmentsRef = useRef<ContentSegment[]>([]);
    31→  const currentTextRef = useRef('');
    32→  const sessionIdRef = useRef<string | null>(null);
    33→  const pendingHistoryRef = useRef<{ role: string; content: string }[] | null>(null);
    34→
    35→  const flushText = useCallback(() => {
    36→    const text = textBufferRef.current;
    37→    if (!text) return;
    38→    const id = assistantIdRef.current;
    39→    currentTextRef.current += text;
    40→    setMessages((prev) =>
    41→      prev.map((m) =>
    42→        m.id === id ? { ...m, content: m.content + text } : m
    43→      )
    44→    );
    45→    textBufferRef.current = '';
    46→  }, []);
    47→
    48→  const sendMessage = useCallback(
    49→    async (text: string) => {
    50→      if (isStreaming) return;
    51→
    52→      const userMsg: ChatMessage = {
    53→        id: generateId(),
    54→        role: 'user',
    55→        content: text,
    56→      };
    57→
    58→      const assistantId = generateId();
    59→      assistantIdRef.current = assistantId;
    60→      const assistantMsg: ChatMessage = {
    61→        id: assistantId,
    62→        role: 'assistant',
    63→        content: '',
    64→        toolCalls: [],
    65→        isStreaming: true,
    66→      };
    67→
    68→      setMessages((prev) => [...prev, userMsg, assistantMsg]);
    69→      setIsStreaming(true);
    70→      textBufferRef.current = '';
    71→      segmentsRef.current = [];
    72→      currentTextRef.current = '';
    73→
    74→      // If there's pending history from a delete, start a new Langfuse session with that context
    75→      const pendingHistory = pendingHistoryRef.current;
    76→      pendingHistoryRef.current = null;
    77→      const langfuseSessionId = pendingHistory ? crypto.randomUUID() : null;
    78→
    79→      const controller = new AbortController();
    80→      abortRef.current = controller;
    81→
    82→      await runAgentLoop(
    83→        text,
    84→        sessionIdRef.current,
    85→        langfuseSessionId,
    86→        pendingHistory,
    87→        {
    88→          onTextChunk: (chunk) => {
    89→            textBufferRef.current += chunk;
    90→            if (!flushTimerRef.current) {
    91→              flushTimerRef.current = setTimeout(() => {
    92→                flushText();
    93→                flushTimerRef.current = null;
    94→              }, 50);
    95→            }
    96→          },
    97→          onThinkingDone: () => {
    98→            // Extended thinking just ended and a text block is starting.
    99→            // Create a thinking segment from accumulated thinking text.
   100→            if (flushTimerRef.current) {
   101→              clearTimeout(flushTimerRef.current);
   102→              flushTimerRef.current = null;
   103→            }
   104→            flushText();
   105→            if (currentTextRef.current.trim()) {
   106→              segmentsRef.current.push({ type: 'thinking', text: currentTextRef.current });
   107→              currentTextRef.current = '';
   108→            }
   109→            setMessages((prev) =>
   110→              prev.map((m) =>
   111→                m.id === assistantId
   112→                  ? { ...m, currentPhase: 'answer', segments: [...segmentsRef.current] }
   113→                  : m
   114→              )
   115→            );
   116→          },
   117→          onToolCall: (pending: ToolCallResult) => {
   118→            if (flushTimerRef.current) {
   119→              clearTimeout(flushTimerRef.current);
   120→              flushTimerRef.current = null;
   121→            }
   122→            flushText();
   123→            if (currentTextRef.current.trim()) {
   124→              segmentsRef.current.push({ type: 'thinking', text: currentTextRef.current });
   125→              currentTextRef.current = '';
   126→            }
   127→            // Add a pending tool segment so the input is shown immediately
   128→            segmentsRef.current.push({
   129→              type: 'tool',
   130→              toolResult: pending,
   131→            });
   132→            setMessages((prev) =>
   133→              prev.map((m) =>
   134→                m.id === assistantId
   135→                  ? { ...m, segments: [...segmentsRef.current] }
   136→                  : m
   137→              )
   138→            );
   139→          },
   140→          onToolResult: (result: ToolCallResult) => {
   141→            // Merge result into pending tool segment (keep input info, add output)
   142→            const pendingIdx = segmentsRef.current.findIndex(
   143→              (s) => s.type === 'tool' && s.toolResult?.toolCallId === result.toolCallId
   144→            );
   145→            if (pendingIdx !== -1) {
   146→              const pending = segmentsRef.current[pendingIdx].toolResult!;
   147→              segmentsRef.current[pendingIdx] = {
   148→                type: 'tool',
   149→                toolResult: {
   150→                  ...pending,
   151→                  ...result,
   152→                  sql: result.sql || pending.sql,
   153→                  toolName: result.toolName || pending.toolName,
   154→                  command: result.command || pending.command,
   155→                  toolInput: result.toolInput || pending.toolInput,
   156→                },
   157→              };
   158→            } else {
   159→              segmentsRef.current.push({ type: 'tool', toolResult: result });
   160→            }
   161→            setMessages((prev) =>
   162→              prev.map((m) =>
   163→                m.id === assistantId
   164→                  ? { ...m, toolCalls: [...(m.toolCalls || []), result], segments: [...segmentsRef.current] }
   165→                  : m
   166→              )
   167→            );
   168→            refreshTables();
   169→          },
   170→          onDone: (newSessionId) => {
   171→            if (newSessionId) sessionIdRef.current = newSessionId;
   172→            if (flushTimerRef.current) {
   173→              clearTimeout(flushTimerRef.current);
   174→              flushTimerRef.current = null;
   175→            }
   176→            flushText();
   177→            if (currentTextRef.current.trim()) {
   178→              segmentsRef.current.push({
   179→                type: 'answer',
   180→                text: currentTextRef.current,
   181→              });
   182→              currentTextRef.current = '';
   183→            }
   184→            setMessages((prev) =>
   185→              prev.map((m) =>
   186→                m.id === assistantId
   187→                  ? { ...m, isStreaming: false, currentPhase: undefined, segments: [...segmentsRef.current] }
   188→                  : m
   189→              )
   190→            );
   191→            setIsStreaming(false);
   192→            abortRef.current = null;
   193→          },
   194→          onError: (error) => {
   195→            if (flushTimerRef.current) {
   196→              clearTimeout(flushTimerRef.current);
   197→              flushTimerRef.current = null;
   198→            }
   199→            flushText();
   200→            setMessages((prev) =>
   201→              prev.map((m) =>
   202→                m.id === assistantId
   203→                  ? { ...m, content: m.content + `\n\n**Error:** ${error}`, isStreaming: false }
   204→                  : m
   205→              )
   206→            );
   207→            setIsStreaming(false);
   208→            abortRef.current = null;
   209→          },
   210→        },
   211→        controller.signal,
   212→        userSessionId,
   213→      );
   214→    },
   215→    [isStreaming, flushText, refreshTables, userSessionId]
   216→  );
   217→

Two distinct bugs. I can see both clearly in the code:

**Bug 1 — Charts in thinking block:** `MessageBubble.tsx:36` filters all non-`answer` segments into the thinking block — that includes `generate_chart` tool results.

**Bug 2 — Empty charts:** `ChartWidget.tsx` uses the factory pattern with `plotly.js-dist-min`, which Vite may not resolve correctly as an ESM default export, so `createPlotlyComponent` gets an empty object.

Fixing both now.

> AGENT

Both fixes committed. Here's what changed:

**Empty charts** — the factory pattern (`createPlotlyComponent(Plotly)` + `plotly.js-dist-min`) doesn't work reliably with Vite's ESM handling. Switched to the direct `import Plot from 'react-plotly.js'` which always works. The bundle is slightly larger but charts will actually render.

**Charts in thinking steps** — `MessageBubble.tsx` was putting all tool segments into the collapsible thinking block. Now chart segments are filtered out of the thinking block and rendered as standalone blocks in the answer area, alongside the agent's text response.
